# Cardy’s formula for critical Poisson–Voronoi percolation

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-C-ML-M-0441` | `PM-224.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Cardys-formula-for-critical-Poisson-Voronoi-percolation-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Cardys-formula-for-critical-Poisson-Voronoi-percolation-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). {
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove Cardy's formula for annealed crossing probabilities in critical planar Poisson–Voronoi percolation in every bounded Jordan quadrilateral. This proves the annealed crossing-probability form of the conformal-invariance conjecture for this model.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Cardy's formula for annealed crossing probabilities in critical planar Poisson–Voronoi percolation in every bounded Jordan quadrilateral. This proves the annealed crossing-probability form of the conformal-invariance conjecture for this model.

- **claim-annealed-cardy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cardy)): For every bounded Jordan domain $D$ and every four distinct counterclockwise boundary marks $a,b,c,d$, one has $$\label{eq:cardy} \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)\bigr) =F(x) :=\frac{\displaystyle\int_0^x[u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,\mathrm du} {\displaystyle\int_0^1[u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,\mathrm du} =\frac{\Gamma(2/3)x^{1/3}} {\Gamma(1/3)\Gamma(4/3)} {}_2F_1\!\left(\frac13,\frac23;\frac43;x\right).$$
  - Explanation: The annealed closed-cell crossing probability converges to Cardy's function of the marked conformal parameter in every bounded Jordan quadrilateral.
- **claim-quenched-rectangles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles)): Let $D$ be a fixed bounded nondegenerate open rectangle and let $a,b,c,d$ be its corners in counterclockwise order, with $ab$ and $cd$ the chosen opposite sides. Write $x_D$ for its conformal parameter above and set $$Q_\varepsilon(\eta)=\mathbb P^{\eta}\bigl(\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)\bigr), \qquad \eta=X_\varepsilon.$$ If $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}$ and $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}$ denote probability and expectation for the Poisson geometry at mesh $\varepsilon$, then $$\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}\bigl[\|Q_\varepsilon-F(x_D)\|^2\bigr]=0.$$ In particular, for every $t>0$, $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}(\|Q_\varepsilon-F(x_D)\|>t)\to0$. These limits use the geometry law at each mesh, without a coupling across meshes.
  - Explanation: For a fixed rectangle, conditional crossing probabilities converge in mean square and in probability over the Poisson geometry. No coupling between meshes is required.
- **claim-two-defect-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fields-defects)): For distinct edge-interior points $m,n$, subdivide the dual edges at these points and take a uniform subgraph with odd degree at $m,n$ and even degree elsewhere. Then $K(m,n)$ is the expected number of circuits in this subgraph. The assertion also applies to distinct points on the same edge.
  - Explanation: The component-connectivity kernel equals the expected circuit count in the uniform defect ensemble, including distinct marks on a common edge.
- **claim-finite-field-orthogonality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fields-orthogonality-prop)): For distinct triangles $t,u$, $$\label{fields:orthogonal} \langle Z_t,Z_u\rangle=0, \qquad \\|Z_t\\|^2=\frac34.$$
  - Explanation: Fourier defect fields at distinct triangles are orthogonal, with an exact common diagonal norm.
- **claim-component-rims** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-rim)): If $C$ contains a dual vertex, the union of the closed primal triangles whose centers belong to $C$ has one simple monochromatic boundary circuit $P$. Orient $P$ with $C$ on its left. Its right-hand triangles are exactly the tags of $(L,C)$, one per edge, and their facing third vertices belong to one cluster of the opposite color. The oriented circuit $P$ determines $(L,C)$.* *If $C$ contains no dual vertex, it is an isolated open edge. It has exactly two tags. When they are in one plane chart, their contributions satisfy $$\label{fields:isolated} V_{(L,C)}(g)=\bigl(g(z_t)-g(z_u)\bigr)d_{te}, \qquad V_{(L,C)}(1)=0,$$ where $t,u$ are its endpoint triangles and $e$ their common missing side.
  - Explanation: A component containing a dual vertex determines a unique oriented monochromatic rim and its right-hand tags. A component without a dual vertex is an isolated open edge whose tangent contributions cancel for constant weights.
- **claim-rim-no-right-bypass** (classification, [source](#stmt-lemma-no-bypass)): A rim has no path of its own color joining two distinct rim vertices with all its other points in the open right-hand disk. Conversely, a simple monochromatic circuit with this property, and with an opposite-colored site in its right-hand disk, is a rim.
  - Explanation: Rims forbid monochromatic bypasses in their open right-hand disk. Conversely, this extremality property identifies a rim when the right-hand disk contains an opposing site.
- **claim-annular-sandwich** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-sandwich)): In an annular band, suppose that there are disjoint essential simple black and white circuits, with the white circuit on the right of the black one for the direction of the band. There is a black rim, with this direction, in the closed region between them.
  - Explanation: Oppositely colored essential circuits with the prescribed side order trap an oriented black rim between them.
- **claim-closed-rim-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-fields-closed-prop)): For every fixed bounded box $B\subset\mathbb C$ and every $a>0$, the full-plane model of spacing $\varepsilon$ satisfies $$\label{fields:closed} \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \mathbb E\!\sum_{\substack{P\text{ a rim},\ P\subset B\\\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(P)\ge a}} \|Q_P(1)\|^2=0.$$ The corresponding full sums for $V_P$ vanish identically.
  - Explanation: The expected squared antilinear mass of complete macroscopic rims in a fixed box vanishes in the microscopic limit. Complete tangent sums vanish exactly.
- **claim-critical-probability-input** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-probability-input)): The following estimates hold at criticality.* 1. *For each fixed rectangle aspect ratio, both colors have crossing probabilities bounded below by a positive constant, uniformly in its size. At sizes tending to infinity, the corresponding quenched crossing probabilities are bounded below with probability tending to one. More precisely, for each fixed finite family of buffered rectangle chains on fixed square grids, and each $\gamma>0$, the required quenched crossing lower bounds hold outside a geometry event of probability $O_\gamma(R^{-\gamma})$ when the family is dilated by $R$. The positive lower bound may depend on the family and on $\gamma$.* 2. *For every fixed $j\ge1$, there is $C_j<\infty$ such that, for all $1\le r\le R$, $$\label{prob:quenched-arms} \mathbb E\bigl[p_j^\eta(r,R)^2\bigr] \le C_j\alpha_j(r,R)^2.$$* 3. *There are constants $c_1,c_4>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for all $1\le r\le R$, $$\label{prob:arm-powers} \alpha_1(r,R)\le C(r/R)^{c_1},\qquad \alpha_5(r,R)\le C(r/R)^2,\qquad \alpha_4(r,R)\le C(r/R)^{1+c_4}.$$
  - Explanation: The established input supplies fixed-shape crossing lower bounds, exceptional-geometry estimates, quenched arm second moments, and annealed arm power bounds at criticality.
- **claim-fixed-geometry-disjoint-witnesses** (bound, [source](#stmt-prob-reimer)): For events $A,B$ in a finite product of fair bits, let $A\mathbin\square B$ mean that there are disjoint sets of coordinates certifying $A$ and $B$. Then $$\label{prob:disjoint-product} \mathbb P(A\mathbin\square B)\le\mathbb P(A)\mathbb P(B).$$ In particular this inequality applies to bounded percolation tests conditional on the Voronoi geometry, with disjoint used site sets.
  - Explanation: Disjoint certificates satisfy a product probability bound in a finite fair-bit space. Its percolation application is conditional on the point geometry.
- **claim-poisson-mesh-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-mesh)): The following facts hold for the full-plane model of spacing $\varepsilon$.* 1. *On a fixed compact set, the Delaunay triangulation and the Voronoi cells meeting it are determined by the points in any fixed positive collar, outside an event of probability tending to zero. Their local diameters tend to zero in probability. The same determination statement holds when the outside point configuration is changed while the collar is retained.* 2. *For a unit square at spacing one, the probability that an incident triangle has circumradius greater than $R>2$ is at most $Ce^{-cR^2}$. The same form of bound holds for a Voronoi cell meeting the square whose site has distance greater than $R$ from it. Counts of incident triangles and sums of any fixed polynomial diameter weights have moments of all orders.* 3. *If $S$ is a square of side $s$, $k\ge0$, and $1\le p<\infty$, then $$\label{prob:mesh-moments} \left\\|\sum_{t\cap S\ne\varnothing}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k\right\\|_{L^p} \le C_{p,k}\varepsilon^{k-2}(s+\varepsilon)^2.$$ For a deterministic Borel set $E$ of finite area, $$\label{prob:barycenter-intensity} \mathbb E\sum_{z_t\in E}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k =m_k\varepsilon^{k-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(E),\qquad m_k<\infty.$$ At every fixed $s>0$, the sum of $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^2$ over triangles with barycenters in $S$ converges in every finite moment norm to $m_2s^2$ as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The sum over triangles meeting $S$ has the same limit. The corresponding statements hold for translation-covariant weights of degree two bounded by a constant times $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^2$, such as $\|p_t\|^2$ and $\|q_t\|^2$.* 4. *Within fixed positive margins, locally determined simple primal cycles can approximate a fixed circle arbitrarily closely and in its cyclic order. Finitely many separated circles admit disjoint such cycles.
  - Explanation: Poisson void bounds give collar determination, shrinking local diameters, exponential geometric tails, polynomial-weight moments, spatial degree-two limits, and ordered local cycle approximations.
- **claim-hex-indicator-crossings** (construction, [source](#stmt-prob-hex)): In a triangulated disk with four successive vertex sides, either there is a black connection between the first and third sides or there is a white connection between the second and fourth sides. In particular, if a set of vertices $X$ blocks every path between one pair of opposite sides, then $X$ contains a path between the other pair.
  - Explanation: Planar duality forces a crossing of one of the opposing color pairs. Applied to indicator colorings, a vertex blocking set contains a transverse path.
- **claim-ordered-color-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prob-color-order)): Let a finite planar graph be drawn in a rectangle, with independent fair colors on its vertices. For any $k\ge1$, the probability of having $k$ vertex-disjoint crossings between its two end sides, with a prescribed linear sequence of colors, depends only on $k$ and not on that sequence. The assertion also holds for a planar subgraph with deleted vertices.
  - Explanation: For fixed geometry and independent fair colors, the probability of an ordered disjoint crossing family is independent of its prescribed color sequence, including after vertex deletion.
- **claim-mixed-four-arm-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-mixed-four)): For some $c>0$ and $C<\infty$, $$\label{prob:mixed-four} \limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \mathbb P\left(\begin{array}{c} \text{four vertex-disjoint primal arms across }B(x,S)\setminus B(x,s),\\ \text{with both colors represented} \end{array}\right) \le C(s/S)^{1+c}.$$ The four colors need not alternate.
  - Explanation: Mixed-color disjoint arms obey a decay bound stronger than a linear power under the fixed-scale microscopic convention. Alternating order is unnecessary.
- **claim-rim-near-return-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-near-return)): For some $c>0$ and $C<\infty$, and the same fixed-scale convention, $$\label{prob:near-return} \limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \mathbb P\left(\begin{array}{c} \text{some rim has two visits to }B(x,s)\text{ such that both}\\ \text{complementary rim arcs between the visits leave }B(x,S) \end{array}\right) \le C(s/S)^{2+c}.$$ The rim can be part of a global spherical completion. The event is bounded by a local monochromatic-arm test, without a union over rims.
  - Explanation: A rim making separated returns implies a local arm event with stronger decay. The bound covers rims in spherical completions without counting individual rims.
- **claim-two-site-annular-gates** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-two-gates)): Let a finite triangulated annulus have disjoint simple primal boundary cycles. If the maximal number of vertex-disjoint black paths joining its two boundary cycles, including disjoint endpoints, is at most two, then the closed annulus contains an essential simple primal circuit with at most two black vertices. The color-interchanged assertion also holds. If the working annulus has positive margins inside a prescribed collar, the resulting circuit lies within that collar.
  - Explanation: A small vertex cut for black annular crossings yields an essential circuit with a correspondingly small set of black contacts. Working margins keep the circuit inside the prescribed collar.
- **claim-universal-bulk-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bulk-transport)): From every sequence $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ one can extract a subsequence and a complex number $a$, depending only on that subsequence, such that $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}a<1/2$ and the following holds in every bulk patch. If $f\in C_c^\infty(B(x,R))$ and $\overline B(x,6R)$ lies in the patch, then $$\label{transport:bulk} \max_{P:\,P\not\subset B(x,4R)} \|Q_P(f)-aV_P(f)\|\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\to0]{\mathbb P}0.$$ As in the definition of a bulk patch, local tests may be set to zero on a geometry event whose probability tends to zero. The subsequence and scalar are common to all patches and tests.
  - Explanation: A common microscopic subsequence and scalar control the transport residual uniformly over rims leaving the test collar. The scalar and subsequence do not depend on the later patch or test.
- **claim-conditional-connector-bridge** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-bridge)): For every fixed connector and admitted endpoint types, there is $c_e>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$ and almost every pair of usable endpoint states, $$\label{plug:success} \nu(S_e\mid\sigma_s,\sigma_t)\ge c_e.$$ The constants are uniform for rigid copies and for buffered families with a uniformly bounded number of fixed-shape crossing rectangles. Moreover, on good continuation geometry, the white part of $S_e$ has a positive quenched lower bound if the two white side tests each have conditional probability at least $\beta$ given that geometry and the inner states.
  - Explanation: Usable endpoint states give positive annealed connector success. Uniformity is restricted to rigid copies and buffered fixed-shape families; the separate white assertion requires quenched side propensities.
- **claim-confined-cycle-rim** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-cycle-rim)): On a cycle experiment, outside an event of probability tending to zero, there is a rim $P$ of the path color, oriented in the positive longitude, contained in the layer $-2.35<v<.8$. At every selected gate circuit it has exactly two contacts, crossing from the negative side into the interior and then out through the positive side. Its outside path from the first-gate exit at $s$ to the first-gate entry at $t$ lies in $\mathcal B_e$. The inner gate paths occur in the prescribed cyclic order.
  - Explanation: Successful cycle experiments contain a confined oriented rim whose gate contacts and inner passages have the prescribed order, outside a vanishing exceptional event.
- **claim-two-graph-path-consistency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-gate-consistency)): Let $P,P'$ be oriented simple monochromatic rims in their respective embedded triangulations. Suppose their directed subpaths $\gamma, \gamma'$ have common distinct initial and terminal vertices $v,w$ and satisfy these conditions:* 1. *each *complete compared subpath* is present and monochromatic in the other graph, with the same embedding;* 2. *at $v$ there is a shared gate circuit, both incoming rim rays lie strictly in one gate fan, and both outgoing rays lie strictly in the opposite fan;* 3. *the rim orientations and direction of crossing that gate agree.* *Then $\gamma=\gamma'$ as directed edge paths. Each rim needs its no-right-bypass property only in its own graph.
  - Explanation: Mutually present monochromatic compared paths with shared ordered gate contacts must agree. Each rim's extremality is used only in its own graph.
- **claim-fixed-comparison-collars** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-fixed-collar)): For a fixed cycle pattern, every possible confined experiment rim has its inner second-gate path in a fixed compact subset of its state disk. Every possible first-exit-to-next-first-entry path lies in a fixed compact subset of $$D_e=O_s\cup O_t\cup\mathcal B_e.$$ Consequently, if two configurations retain these data, their complete incident triangulations along the compared paths agree on the mesh-good event, for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Fixed cycle geometry supplies deterministic compact envelopes for every possible compared path. Retaining those envelopes preserves complete local incident triangulations on good mesh events.
- **claim-state-cochain** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-state-cochain)): For every fixed connector, including its actual end charts, there are functions $i_e^\varepsilon(\sigma_s,\sigma_t)$ such that $$\label{plug:cycle} \begin{aligned} \widehat m_s+\widehat j_e-i_e^\varepsilon&=o_{L^1}(1) &&\text{in every cycle experiment containing }e,\\ \sum_e i_e^\varepsilon&=o_{L^1}(1) &&\text{on the independent state product of every simple system}. \end{aligned}$$ The same functions work in all completions. For a fixed collection of normalized short templates admitting uniform short completions, $$\\|i_e^\varepsilon\\|_{L^2(\mu_s\otimes\mu_t)}\le C h$$ for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, after increasing a common constant.
  - Explanation: Connector masses become functions of retained endpoint states, common across completions. Their sums around fixed cycles vanish, and uniformly short templates have a common moment bound.
- **claim-common-closure-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-common-closure)): Suppose two directed paths of connectors from $s$ to $t$ admit a common complementary connector $c:t\to s$, making each a simple system. Their sums of cochain values differ by $o_{L^1}(1)$ on the product of all states, with shared states identified. Either path may be a single connector. For fixed end disks and charts, a connector value therefore depends, up to $o_{L^1}(1)$, only on the endpoint-fixed deformation class of its exterior simple centerline described below.
  - Explanation: Subtracting cycle sums with a shared complement compares connector paths on the same state product. The resulting invariance concerns endpoint-fixed deformation classes.
- **claim-prescribed-band-collars** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-band-collar)): Let a simple exterior arc join the middle points of two prescribed port sections. Suppose it continues for a short distance along the given product collars at its ends and otherwise avoids the closed end disks. Then a rectangular band about it, with those full parameterized attachments, fits in any neighborhood of the arc and attachments within the exterior. It can in addition avoid any fixed disjoint compact routes or unused obstacles.
  - Explanation: An exterior simple arc with compatible product end collars admits a surrounding band with the full parameterized port attachments and prescribed compact avoidance.
- **claim-buffered-chart-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-chart-stability)): Fix a buffered two-plug system $e_0,c_0$. Suppose its parts in the preset neighborhoods where charts may move are fixed local stubs, and no other part of the system returns to these neighborhoods. For sufficiently small supported changes $g$ of the two end charts and obstacles, let $e_g,c_g$ be the corresponding images. Then $$i_{e_g}^\varepsilon=i_{e_0}^\varepsilon+o_{L^1}(1) \quad\text{on }\mu_s\otimes\mu_t.$$ The required tolerance depends only on the compact local stub and layer margins in the moved regions.
  - Explanation: Small supported chart changes preserve the cochain asymptotically on buffered local templates with fixed stubs and no remote returns to the moved neighborhoods.
- **claim-affine-straight-means** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-affine)): For $X=Q,V$ there is a scalar $a_h^X$, uniformly bounded in $h$, such that every fixed straight transport in the countable family used below has mean $$\label{affine:means} \mathbb E_\mu i_e^\varepsilon=a_h^X(z_t-z_s)+o(1).$$ The same scalar applies to translations, common rotations and color flips of the reference type. All choices can be made simultaneously for the two fields and countably many $h$.
  - Explanation: Straight connector means are affine in the oriented endpoint displacement, with a uniformly bounded scalar common to translated, rotated, and color-flipped reference types.
- **claim-tangent-scalar-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-tangent-normalization)): The scalar of the tangent field is $$\label{affine:tangent} a_h^V=1.$$
  - Explanation: The tangent transport scalar is exactly normalized by comparing masked displacement sums with geometric contour displacement.
- **claim-endpoint-twist-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-lemma-two-twists)): After normalizing the two disjoint Jordan obstacles to round holes, every exterior simple centerline can be replaced, by common-closure comparisons, by a smooth proper arc radial at its ends. Such arcs with fixed endpoints are classified, for the comparisons needed here, by two integer endpoint twists. Relative to a standard route with bounded endpoint turns, their total size $k$ is bounded by a constant plus a constant times the sum of the absolute net argument changes about the two centers along the exterior arc.
  - Explanation: After normalizing the obstacles, smooth radial-ended connector classes are recorded by integer endpoint twists. Their size is controlled by net argument changes, rather than total arclength.
- **claim-buffered-standard-spirals** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-standard-spirals)): Fix nominal terminal placements, their types and two twist integers $n_s,n_t$, and put $k=\|n_s\|+\|n_t\|$. There is a constant $C$ such that, if $$\|z_t-z_s\|>Ch(1+k),$$ the class has a buffered representative beginning and ending with the fixed insertions above. Between them it consists of straight transports and at most $C(1+k)$ short pieces. The short pieces belong, up to rigid motion, to uniformly buffered compact families of bounded normalized size, and satisfy $$\\|i_j^\varepsilon\\|_{L^2}\le C_{\rm short}h$$ for small $\varepsilon$, with a constant independent of the terminal separation, the twists and the particular long connector.* *A common complementary connector closes both the inserted path and its unsplit representative. All auxiliary placements and straight lengths can be fixed from the nominal terminal data and integers before making allowed chart changes. In the applications they form a countable list.
  - Explanation: Sufficiently separated terminals admit a buffered representative made of straight transports and controlled short corner pieces, with a common complementary connector and predetermined auxiliary placements.
- **claim-local-endpoint-potentials** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint-potentials)): For every admitted departure or arrival placement and type there are centered state functions $\phi_s^{X,+}$ and $\phi_t^{X,-}$, allowed to depend on $\varepsilon$, with $$\\|\phi_s^{X,+}\\|_{L^2(\mu_s)}\le C_{\rm short}h, \qquad \\|\phi_t^{X,-}\\|_{L^2(\mu_t)}\le C_{\rm short}h.$$ They depend only on the indicated endpoint data, and not on the long connector or its allowed small chart changes. For each fixed connector $e:s\to t$ of class size $k$, provided $\|z_t-z_s\|>Ch(1+k)$, $$\label{affine:transport} \begin{split} i_e^\varepsilon(\sigma_s,\sigma_t) ={}&a_h^X(z_t-z_s) +\phi_s^{X,+}(\sigma_s)+\phi_t^{X,-}(\sigma_t) +b_e^\varepsilon+\rho_e^\varepsilon,\\ &\\|\rho_e^\varepsilon\\|_{L^1(\mu_s\otimes\mu_t)}\longrightarrow0, \qquad \limsup_{\varepsilon\to0}\|b_e^\varepsilon\|\le C_{\rm short}h(1+k). \end{split}$$ Here $b_e^\varepsilon$ is deterministic. The constants depend on the fixed normalized menu and short templates, but not on a thin fitted connector, its length or a finite cover of such connectors.
  - Explanation: Connector values split into affine displacement, centered local endpoint potentials, a deterministic twist-dependent bias, and a vanishing mean error. Uniform constants come from short templates.
- **claim-profile-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-profile-compactness)): Every sequence of the above profiles satisfying increasingly long initial lists of the inverse-diameter constraints has a subsequence converging in marked Fréchet and Hausdorff distance to a simple marked arc $K$ and a compact connected set $E$. The required marks remain distinct. In the limit all visits of $K$ to $\overline B(0,4b_2)$ occur in the interior of its marked middle interval $\Delta\subset\overline B(0,b_3)$. The set $E$ misses $\overline B(0,4b_2)$ and misses $K\cap B(0,6L)$. The family of all such limiting profiles is compact.
  - Explanation: Increasing inverse-diameter constraints yield simple marked arc limits and connected complementary compact sets. The constraints preserve mark separation and exclude unwanted inner intersections.
- **claim-exact-profile-fitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-exact-fit)): Each limiting profile in Lemma <a href="#lemma:profile-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:profile-compactness">38</a> has a nominal plug model, fixed local stubs, a positive allowed chart-change tolerance, and an open fit neighborhood with the following properties. Every fitting prelimit traversal has the strict tracking properties in Definition <a href="#pin:qualification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pin:qualification">37</a>. For every simple profile in the closure of this neighborhood there is an orientation-preserving ambient homeomorphism $g$, supported in $B(0,50L)$ and within the allowed tolerance, such that its ordered arc agrees *exactly* with $g\Phi(u,0)$ through $gH$ and continues along it to $\|u\|=9.5$. There are no other visits of the arc or its unwanted set $E$ to $gH$. A finite collection of such neighborhoods contains all profiles satisfying a sufficiently long finite initial list of constraints.
  - Explanation: Limiting profiles admit nominal plugs with positive fitting tolerance. Nearby simple profiles fit exactly after a small supported ambient change, and a finite menu covers sufficiently constrained profiles.
- **claim-dense-qualified-pins** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-dense-pins)): There exist a finite normalized menu and fixed $\beta,\beta'>0$ such that the following holds in every bulk patch with $\overline B(x,6R)$ available. With probability tending to one in the ordered limit $\varepsilon\to0$, then $h\to0$, every subarc of diameter at least $r_h$ in $\overline B(x,2R)$ on a rim reaching outside $B(x,4R)$ contains a qualified pin. The statement holds simultaneously for all such subarcs and both colors. It also holds for the coupled bulk settings on their patch-agreement event.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite menu and fixed thresholds supply qualified pins on every eligible macroscopic rim subarc in the ordered limit, simultaneously for both colors and coupled bulk settings.
- **claim-dominated-forcing-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-dominated-kernel)): Let $\nu$ be a probability measure on configurations, let $Z$ be retained data, and let $\nu_z$ be a regular conditional law given $Z=z$. Suppose $H$ is a measurable set of retained data and $p(z)=\nu_z(W)\ge q>0$ on $H$. Let $K$ fix $Z$ and satisfy $\nu_zK_z=\nu_z(\cdot\mid W)$ there. If an unnormalized subprobability $\lambda$ satisfies $\lambda\le C\nu$ and is supported over $H$, then $$\lambda K\le (C/q)\nu.$$ For the additional monotone assertion, suppose that, conditional on $Z=z$, the remaining coordinates have law $\nu_z=\pi_z\otimes\tau_z$, where $\pi_z$ is a product spin law on a finite, $Z$-measurable set $I(z)$ of exterior sites and $\tau_z$ is the law of the complementary data. Suppose also that $W$ depends only on $Z$ and the spins in $I(Z)$ and is increasing in white on those spins. Then $K$ can be chosen to recolor only existing black sites in $I(Z)$ white and to fix $Z$ and every complementary coordinate.
  - Explanation: A forcing kernel with uniformly positive ordinary conditional success preserves bounded domination of an unnormalized selected subprobability. A separate product-spin hypothesis permits monotone recoloring while fixing complementary data.
- **claim-retained-triangulation-collars** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-retained-collar)): Fix $h$ and finitely many deterministic path envelopes and state disks. Suppose the point data are kept on a deterministic Borel set containing an open collar of every envelope and of the state portions under comparison. Set $\rho_\varepsilon=\sqrt{h\varepsilon}$. For small $\varepsilon$, every Delaunay triangle of circumradius at most $\rho_\varepsilon$ meeting these envelopes, together with its incident edge and tag data there, is determined by the kept point restriction. Thus two configurations with the same restriction have the same such local triangulations.* *In a fixed two-plug comparison one can keep the two endpoint states and the whole exterior sector of the used connector. The inset paths of the cycle experiment and any sufficiently closely tracking actual arc, including their inner second-gate paths, then have the common triangulations just described on the ordinary mesh-good events. The complementary connector can be resampled without altering them.
  - Explanation: Kept open collars determine triangles below the retained circumradius threshold and their local incident data. Keeping the used sector and states permits resampling the complementary connector without changing compared paths.
- **claim-rim-winding-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-winding)): Except on events of probability tending to zero in the ordered limit, every subarc of a rim in $B(x,3R)$ that stays at distance at least $h$ from a grid center in use has absolute net change of continuous argument about that center at most $C_R(1+\|\log h\|^2)$.
  - Explanation: Annular crossing tails bound the absolute net argument change of every relevant rim subarc away from the grid center, outside ordered-limit exceptions.
- **claim-real-arc-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-real-arc)): Simultaneously for $X=Q,V$ and every qualified ordered arc from $s$ to $t$ contained in $\overline B(x,2R)$, with $\|z_s-z_t\|\ge R/3$ and complementary rim reaching outside $B(x,4R)$, one has $$\label{actual:arc} \left\|I_X(P;s,t)-a_h^X(z_t-z_s) -\phi_s^{X,+}(\sigma'_s)-\phi_t^{X,-}(\sigma'_t)\right\| \le C_Rh(1+\|\log h\|^2)$$ outside events whose probability tends to zero in the ordered limit. The constant is independent of the complexity and conditioning constants of the individual fixed comparison patterns.
  - Explanation: The affine and endpoint-potential formula transfers uniformly to qualified arcs in the original configuration. Pattern-specific forcing constants affect vanishing errors, not the uniform deterministic bias.
- **claim-mixed-atom-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-mixed-atoms)): Use the preceding excursion and pin assignments, and choose $0<\kappa<c/2$, decreasing the positive exponent $c$ if necessary. Then, for $X=Q,V$, $$\max_P\left\|X_P(f)-\mathcal A_X(P)\right\| \longrightarrow0$$ in probability in the ordered limit. The maximum is over the same macroscopically reaching rims as above, on the preceding good events.
  - Explanation: Adaptive pin coefficients approximate the signed fields uniformly over macroscopic rims. Deterministic atom refinement, near-return events, and full-label energy bounds provide the needed cancellation control.
- **claim-transport-scalar-nondegeneracy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-nondegeneracy)): Every scalar obtained from the preceding transport construction satisfies $$1-2\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}a>0.$$ In particular, $a\ne1$.
  - Explanation: The positive area imbalance forces the transport scalar to be nondegenerate. Contour replacement and uniform integrability justify the averaged pairing used in the argument.
- **claim-ordered-polygonal-disks** (construction, [source](#stmt-lemma-polygonal-disks)): Let $D$ be a polygonal Jordan domain with finitely many marked boundary points in their cyclic order. With probability tending to one, the Delaunay triangulation contains a simple primal cycle whose boundary and selected marked vertices converge to the marked boundary of $D$ in order and in Fréchet distance. The triangulation inside this cycle agrees with the full-plane triangulation on every fixed compact subset of $D$ for all sufficiently fine good approximations.
  - Explanation: A polygonal marked Jordan boundary is approximated in order by a simple primal cycle, while the interior triangulation retains agreement on fixed compact subsets.
- **claim-triangular-crossing-observable** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-observable)): The value $F_m^\eta$ belongs to the closed triangle with vertices $w_0,w_1,w_2$. At a fan probe $s_i u$, where $u$ is interior to the boundary arc $v_i v_{i+1}$, it belongs to the side $[w_i,w_{i+1}]$. For $i=0$ and $u$ the marked vertex approximating $b$, the coefficient of $w_1$ on this side is exactly the free black vertex crossing probability between the arcs approximating $ab$ and $cd$ in the original disk.
  - Explanation: The closing-star observable is a convex combination of triangle vertices, restricts to prescribed boundary sides, and has a boundary coefficient equal to the original disk's free black crossing probability.
- **claim-geometry-selected-probe-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-probe-compactness)): From every microscopic sequence one can select a subsequence, still satisfying the bulk transport conclusion, on which the annealed observables converge to a continuous function $F$ in $D$ and on its three open boundary arcs. The convergence holds for every geometry-measurable choice of admissible probes tending to a specified point off the three marks. The limit takes values in the closed triangle and has the corresponding closed side values on the open boundary arcs.
  - Explanation: Annealed observables admit continuous subsequential limits in the interior and on open marked boundary arcs. The limit is independent of the geometry-measurable probe selection approaching a fixed unmarked point.
- **claim-limit-observable-holomorphy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-holomorphy)): Every limit $F$ in Lemma <a href="#lemma:probe-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:probe-compactness">49</a> is holomorphic in $D$.
  - Explanation: Every continuous observable limit is holomorphic. Deterministic limits of random edge measures allow the proof to retain correlations between geometry and conditional observables.
- **claim-ordered-boundary-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-boundary-uniqueness)): Let $G:\mathbb D\to\mathbb D$ be nonconstant and holomorphic. Let $\zeta_0,\zeta_1,\zeta_2$ and $\eta_0,\eta_1,\eta_2$ be positively ordered triples of distinct points of the unit circle. Suppose that $G$ extends continuously to each open arc $(\zeta_i,\zeta_{i+1})$ and takes its values there in the corresponding positively oriented closed arc $[\eta_i,\eta_{i+1}]$. Then $G$ extends continuously to the closed disk, maps $\zeta_i$ to $\eta_i$, and is a conformal bijection of the disk.
  - Explanation: A nonconstant holomorphic disk map with the prescribed ordered boundary-arc constraints extends continuously at the marks and is a conformal bijection.
- **claim-fixed-buffer-jordan-crossings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-jordan-squeeze)): Let $D$ be any bounded Jordan domain with distinct positively ordered boundary marks $a,b,c,d$. For the full-plane Poisson tessellation with independent fair cell colors, the annealed probability of a connected crossing in the closed black-cell union intersected with $\overline D$, from $ab$ to $cd$, converges to $\mathcal C(x)$ with the marked conformal parameter of $D$.
  - Explanation: Fixed harder and easier comparison domains squeeze the exact connected closed-cell crossing event to the conformal crossing value in an arbitrary bounded Jordan domain.

        "exact_quote": "The following estimates hold at criticality.*\n\n1.  *For each fixed rectangle aspect ratio, both colors have crossing probabilities bounded below by a positive constant, uniformly in its size. At sizes tending to infinity, the corresponding quenched crossing probabilities are bounded below with probability tending to one. More precisely, for each fixed finite family of buffered rectangle chains on fixed square grids, and each $\\gamma>0$, the required quenched crossing lower bounds hold outside a geometry event of probability $O_\\gamma(R^{-\\gamma})$ when the family is dilated by $R$. The positive lower bound may depend on the family and on $\\gamma$.*\n\n\n2.  *For every fixed $j\\ge1$, there is $C_j<\\infty$ such that, for all $1\\le r\\le R$, $$\\label{prob:quenched-arms}\n     \\mathbb E\\bigl[p_j^\\eta(r,R)^2\\bigr]\n           \\le C_j\\alpha_j(r,R)^2.$$*\n\n\n3.  *There are constants $c_1,c_4>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for all $1\\le r\\le R$, $$\\label{prob:arm-powers}\n     \\alpha_1(r,R)\\le C(r/R)^{c_1},\\qquad\n     \\alpha_5(r,R)\\le C(r/R)^2,\\qquad\n     \\alpha_4(r,R)\\le C(r/R)^{1+c_4}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-probability-input",
          "exact_quote": "The following estimates hold at criticality.*\n\n1.  *For each fixed rectangle aspect ratio, both colors have crossing probabilities bounded below by a positive constant, uniformly in its size.         "anchor": "stmt-prob-reimer",
        "exact_quote": "For events $A,B$ in a finite product of fair bits, let $A\\mathbin\\square B$ mean that there are disjoint sets of coordinates certifying $A$ and $B$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-mesh",
        "exact_quote": "The following facts hold for the full-plane model of spacing $\\varepsilon$.*\n\n1.  *On a fixed compact set, the Delaunay triangulation and the Voronoi cells meeting it are determined by the points in any fixed positive collar, outside an event of probability tending to zero. Their local diameters tend to zero in probability. The same determination statement holds when the outside point configuration is changed while the collar is retained.*\n\n2.  *For a unit square at spacing one, the probability that an incident triangle has circumradius greater than $R>2$ is at most $Ce^{-cR^2}$. The same form of bound holds for a Voronoi cell meeting the square whose site has distance greater than $R$ from it. Counts of incident triangles and sums of any fixed polynomial diameter weights have moments of all orders.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $S$ is a square of side $s$, $k\\ge0$, and $1\\le p<\\infty$, then $$\\label{prob:mesh-moments}\n     \\left\\|\\sum_{t\\cap S\\ne\\varnothing}\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k\\right\\|_{L^p}\n       \\le C_{p,k}\\varepsilon^{k-2}(s+\\varepsilon)^2.$$ For a deterministic Borel set $E$ of finite area, $$\\label{prob:barycenter-intensity}\n     \\mathbb E\\sum_{z_t\\in E}\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k\n        =m_k\\varepsilon^{k-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}(E),\\qquad m_k<\\infty.$$ At every fixed $s>0$, the sum of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}(t)^2$ over triangles with barycenters in $S$ converges in every finite moment norm to $m_2s^2$ as $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$. The sum over triangles meeting $S$ has the same limit. The corresponding statements hold for translation-covariant weights of degree two bounded by a constant times $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}(t)^2$, such as $|p_t|^2$ and $|q_t|^2$.*\n\n4.  *Within fixed positive margins, locally determined simple primal cycles can approximate a fixed circle arbitrarily closely and in its cyclic order. Finitely many separated circles admit disjoint such cycles.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },

        {

The sum over triangles meeting $S$ has the same limit. Finitely many separated circles admit disjoint such cycles.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-mixed-four",
        "exact_quote": "For some $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$, $$\\label{prob:mixed-four}\n \\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n \\mathbb P\\left(\\begin{array}{c}\n \\text{four vertex-disjoint primal arms across }B(x,S)\\setminus B(x,s),\\\\\n \\text{with both colors represented}\n \\end{array}\\right)\n \\le C(s/S)^{1+c}.$$ The four colors need not alternate.",

      "explanation": "Mixed-color disjoint arms obey a decay bound stronger than a linear power under the fixed-scale microscopic convention. Alternating order is unnecessary.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-gates",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite triangulated annulus have disjoint simple primal boundary cycles. If the maximal number of vertex-disjoint black paths joining its two boundary cycles, including disjoint endpoints, is at most two, then the closed annulus contains an essential simple primal circuit with at most two black vertices. The color-interchanged assertion also holds. If the working annulus has positive margins inside a prescribed collar, the resulting circuit lies within that collar.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-gates",
          "exact_quote": "Let a finite triangulated annulus have disjoint simple primal boundary cycles. The color-interchanged assertion also holds. If the working annulus has positive margins inside a prescribed collar, the resulting circuit lies within that collar.",
          "source_id": "PM-224.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let a finite triangulated annulus have disjoint simple primal boundary cycles.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed connector and admitted endpoint types, there is $c_e>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\varepsilon$ and almost every pair of usable endpoint states, $$\\label{plug:success}\n \\nu(S_e\\mid\\sigma_s,\\sigma_t)\\ge c_e.$$ The constants are uniform for rigid copies and for buffered families with a uniformly bounded number of fixed-shape crossing rectangles.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-gate-consistency",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P,P'$ be oriented simple monochromatic rims in their respective embedded triangulations.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-chart-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a buffered two-plug system $e_0,c_0$. Suppose its parts in the preset neighborhoods where charts may move are fixed local stubs, and no other part of the system returns to these neighborhoods. For sufficiently small supported changes $g$ of the two end charts and obstacles, let $e_g,c_g$ be the corresponding images.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-twists",
        "exact_quote": "After normalizing the two disjoint Jordan obstacles to round holes, every exterior simple centerline can be replaced, by common-closure comparisons, by a smooth proper arc radial at its ends.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-standard-spirals",
        "exact_quote": "Fix nominal terminal placements, their types and two twist integers $n_s,n_t$, and put $k=|n_s|+|n_t|$. There is a constant $C$ such that, if $$|z_t-z_s|>Ch(1+k),$$ the class has a buffered representative beginning and ending with the fixed insertions above. Between them it consists of straight transports and at most $C(1+k)$ short pieces. The short pieces belong, up to rigid motion, to uniformly buffered compact families of bounded normalized size, and satisfy $$\\|i_j^\\varepsilon\\|_{L^2}\\le C_{\\rm short}h$$ for small $\\varepsilon$, with a constant independent of the terminal separation, the twists and the particular long connector.*\n\n*A common complementary connector closes both the inserted path and its unsplit representative.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-profile-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Every sequence of the above profiles satisfying increasingly long initial lists of the inverse-diameter constraints has a subsequence converging in marked Fréchet and Hausdorff distance to a simple marked arc $K$ and a compact connected set $E$.         "anchor": "stmt-lemma-exact-fit",
        "exact_quote": "Each limiting profile in Lemma <a href=\"#lemma:profile-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:profile-compactness\">38</a> has a nominal plug model, fixed local stubs, a positive allowed chart-change tolerance, and an open fit neighborhood with the following properties. Every fitting prelimit traversal has the strict tracking properties in Definition <a href=\"#pin:qualification\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pin:qualification\">37</a>. For every simple profile in the closure of this neighborhood there is an orientation-preserving ambient homeomorphism $g$, supported in $B(0,50L)$ and within the allowed tolerance, such that its ordered arc agrees *exactly* with $g\\Phi(u,0)$ through $gH$ and continues along it to $|u|=9.5$. There are no other visits of the arc or its unwanted set $E$ to $gH$. A finite collection of such neighborhoods contains all profiles satisfying a sufficiently long finite initial list of constraints.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-dense-pins",
        "exact_quote": "There exist a finite normalized menu and fixed $\\beta,\\beta'>0$ such that the following holds in every bulk patch with $\\overline B(x,6R)$ available. With probability tending to one in the ordered limit $\\varepsilon\\to0$, then $h\\to0$, every subarc of diameter at least $r_h$ in $\\overline B(x,2R)$ on a rim reaching outside $B(x,4R)$ contains a qualified pin. The statement holds simultaneously for all such subarcs and both colors. It also holds for the coupled bulk settings on their patch-agreement event.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dense-pins",

        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-dominated-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu$ be a probability measure on configurations, let $Z$ be retained data, and let $\\nu_z$ be a regular conditional law given $Z=z$. Suppose $H$ is a measurable set of retained data and $p(z)=\\nu_z(W)\\ge q>0$ on $H$. Let $K$ fix $Z$ and satisfy $\\nu_zK_z=\\nu_z(\\cdot\\mid W)$ there. If an unnormalized subprobability $\\lambda$ satisfies $\\lambda\\le C\\nu$ and is supported over $H$, then $$\\lambda K\\le (C/q)\\nu.$$ For the additional monotone assertion, suppose that, conditional on $Z=z$, the remaining coordinates have law $\\nu_z=\\pi_z\\otimes\\tau_z$, where $\\pi_z$ is a product spin law on a finite, $Z$-measurable set $I(z)$ of exterior sites and $\\tau_z$ is the law of the complementary data. Suppose also that $W$ depends only on $Z$ and the spins in $I(Z)$ and is increasing in white on those spins. Then $K$ can be chosen to recolor only existing black sites in $I(Z)$ white and to fix $Z$ and every complementary coordinate.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-retained-collar",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $h$ and finitely many deterministic path envelopes and state disks. Suppose the point data are kept on a deterministic Borel set containing an open collar of every envelope and of the state portions under comparison. Set $\\rho_\\varepsilon=\\sqrt{h\\varepsilon}$. For small $\\varepsilon$, every Delaunay triangle of circumradius at most $\\rho_\\varepsilon$ meeting these envelopes, together with its incident edge and tag data there, is determined by the kept point restriction. Thus two configurations with the same restriction have the same such local triangulations.*\n\n*In a fixed two-plug comparison one can keep the two endpoint states and the whole exterior sector of the used connector. The inset paths of the cycle experiment and any sufficiently closely tracking actual arc, including their inner second-gate paths, then have the common triangulations just described on the ordinary mesh-good events. The complementary connector can be resampled without altering them.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-probe-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "From every microscopic sequence one can select a subsequence, still satisfying the bulk transport conclusion, on which the annealed observables converge to a continuous function $F$ in $D$ and on its three open boundary arcs. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **critical Poisson–Voronoi model and probability laws** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $\varepsilon>0$, let $\eta=X_\varepsilon$ be a homogeneous Poisson point process of intensity $\varepsilon^{-2}$. For $v\in\eta$ its Voronoi cell is the closed set $$\mathcal V_v=\{z\in\mathbb C:\|z-v\|\le \|z-u\|\text{ for every }u\in\eta\}.$$ Independently of $\eta$, color its sites black or white with probability $1/2$ each, and give each cell the color of its site. Let $\mathcal B_\varepsilon$ be the union of the closed black cells. Probabilities and expectations under the joint law of the points and colors are denoted by $\mathbb P$ and $\mathbb E$. Conditional probabilities given the points are denoted by $\mathbb P^{\eta}$; $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}$ denotes averaging over independent fair colors on a fixed graph. Thus all probabilities in the main theorem are annealed.
- **closed-cell crossing event** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Define $\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)$ to be the event that $\mathcal B_\varepsilon\cap\overline D$ contains a connected subset meeting both the arc $ab$ and the arc $cd$.
- **marked conformal parameter** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The conformal map from $D$ to $\mathbb H=\{z:\operatorname{Im}z>0\}$ which sends $a,c,d$ to $0,1,\infty$ extends to the boundary in the sense of Carathéodory (Pommerenke 1992, vol. 299, sec. 2.1, p. 18) [[Pommerenke1992](#ref-Pommerenke1992)]. Its value at $b$ is a uniquely determined number $x\in(0,1)$.
- **bulk setting** ([source](#stmt-def-bulk-setting)): A bulk setting is either the full-plane sample or a finite simple triangulation of the sphere carrying independent fair site colors conditional on its graph and geometry. In the latter case an oriented open planar patch is coupled to a full-plane sample so that, on every fixed compact subset of the patch, the graphs and their colors agree with probability tending to one as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The graph and chart agreement event is independent of color sampling conditional on the geometry. Spatial weights are used only on triangles in the agreement patch. Compactly supported tests may be set to zero on the exceptional geometry event.
- **occupied dual edges** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A dual edge is *occupied* when the endpoints of the primal edge it crosses have different colors.
- **circuit-component labels and kernel** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For each occupied circuit $L$, take the connected components $C$ of the topological graph $\Gamma\setminus L$: all points of $L$, including its vertices, are removed. In particular, an open edge whose two endpoints have been removed can be a component by itself. Figure <a href="#fig:component-labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:component-labels">1</a> illustrates the deletion convention in the tetrahedral dual. Put $\lambda=(L,C)$ and, for a point $m$ in the interior of a dual edge, define $$H_\lambda(m)=\mathbf 1_{\{m\in C\}}.$$ Vectors indexed by these pairs, with the index set taken separately in each configuration, have the scalar product $$\langle F,G\rangle =\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\sum_\lambda F_\lambda\overline{G_\lambda}.$$ Here and throughout the finite discussion, $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}$ averages colors with the triangulation fixed. Write $H(m)=(H_\lambda(m))_\lambda$ and $K(m,n)=\langle H(m),H(n)\rangle$.
- **Fourier defect field** ([source](#pm-h-10)): With $\omega=\exp(2\pi\mathrm i/3)$, set $$Z_t=\sum_{j=0}^2\omega^jH(m_j(t)).$$
- **affine side coefficients** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Since the three side vectors sum to zero, there are unique $p_t,q_t\in\mathbb C$ such that $$\label{fields:side-decomposition} d_{tj}=p_t\omega^j+q_t\overline{\omega}^{j}, \qquad p_t=\frac13\sum_jd_{tj}\omega^{-j}, \quad q_t=\frac13\sum_jd_{tj}\omega^j.$$
- **tagged displacement fields** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A triangle $t$ is *tagged* for $\lambda=(L,C)$ by $j$ if $L$ uses $t$ and its missing spoke $j$ belongs to $C$. For a coefficient function $g$ supported where the side vectors are defined, put $$\begin{aligned} U_\lambda(g)&=\sum_{t\text{ tagged by }j}g(z_t)p_t\omega^j,\\ Q_\lambda(g)&=\sum_{t\text{ tagged by }j}g(z_t)q_t\overline{\omega}^{j}, &V_\lambda(g)&=U_\lambda(g)+Q_\lambda(g). \end{aligned}$$
- **arm events and arm probabilities** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For $1\le r\le R$, let $A_j(r,R)$ be the arm event across the square annulus $[-R,R]^2\setminus(-r,r)^2$. For even $j$ the $j$ arms have alternating colors in cyclic order. For odd $j>1$ there are $j-1$ alternating arms and an additional black arm such that no Voronoi cell intersects both that arm and any other arm. The event $A_1$ is the existence of a black arm. In these definitions paths can be chosen so that their used site sets are disjoint. Set $$\alpha_j(r,R)=\mathbb P(A_j(r,R)), \qquad p_j^\eta(r,R)=\mathbb P^{\eta}(A_j(r,R)).$$
- **comparison-scale order** ([source](#stmt-plug-scale-order)): A comparison scale $h>0$ is fixed before the microscopic limit. Every $o(1)$ and $o_{L^1}(1)$ in this section and the next is taken as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ at fixed $h$ and fixed deterministic geometric pattern, through values for which the stated types are admitted. Later we use a countable sequence $h\downarrow0$. Thus a finite pattern may have an arbitrarily small positive conditioning probability, and the number of patterns may depend on $h$. Bounds of the form $C h$ will instead be obtained from fixed normalized short templates, with a common constant.
- **plug type** ([source](#stmt-def-plug-type)): A type consists of the following deterministic data: 1. two concentric gate annuli $[a_1,b_1]$, $[a_2,b_2]$ and a cutoff radius $m$, with $$10<a_1<b_1<m/10,\qquad 10m<a_2<b_2;$$ 2. closed Jordan disks $O\subset\operatorname{int}H$, with $\overline B(0,b_2)\subset\operatorname{int}O$, and an orientation-preserving band chart $$\Phi:[-12,12]\times[-3,3]\longrightarrow\mathbb R^2.$$ Writing its coordinates as $(u,v)$, membership in $O$ and $H$ within the chart is exactly $\|u\|\le3$ and $\|u\|\le8$, respectively; membership in the interiors is given by the strict inequalities. The full section $\Phi(\{0\}\times[-3,3])$ is strictly inside $B(0,a_1)$; 3. one positive integer rank for each of the two windows $$I_B=[-.3,.3],\qquad I_W=[-1.8,-1.4].$$ No metric regularity of the Jordan disks or chart is imposed.
- **usable states and admission** ([source](#stmt-def-usable-state)): Fix positive thresholds $\beta,\beta'$. The state event $G$ requires: 1. simple surrounding retained site circuits $S_1,S_2$ in the two gate annuli, with their edge segments in those annuli, enclosing the corresponding inner balls and containing at most two black sites each; 2. both roots and the inequalities $\nu(L^+\mid\sigma)\ge\beta$ and $\nu(L^-\mid\sigma)\ge\beta$. The conditional probabilities use independent ordinary data on $O^c$; in particular they average over the exterior point geometry as well as its colors. A type is *admitted* when $\nu(G)\ge\beta'$, and its state law is $$\mu=\nu(\sigma\in\cdot\mid G).$$ The finite normalized menu and thresholds will be fixed in Section <a href="#sec:pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pins">6</a>. Admission and $\mu$ may depend on $h,\varepsilon$.
- **connectors and simple systems** ([source](#stmt-def-simple-system)): A directed connector $e:s\to t$ between same-color plugs with disjoint obstacles is an oriented rectangular band, with transverse parameter $v\in[-3,3]$, attached by its full parameterized ends to the positive port $u_s=8$ of $H_s$ and the negative port $u_t=-8$ of $H_t$. Apart from these attachments it avoids both disks. Only the charts with $\|u\|\le8$ are prescribed at its ends. A *simple system* is a cyclic list of at least two distinct plugs and connectors. Its closed obstacles are pairwise disjoint, its exterior bands are pairwise disjoint and avoid all other obstacles, and the sectors from section $0$ of one plug to section $0$ of the next form an embedded annular band with the prescribed directed longitude and transverse parameter. Denote the whole closed sector of $e$ by $\mathcal B_e$.
- **cycle experiment** ([source](#stmt-def-cycle-experiment)): For a simple system, sample the states independently with their laws $\mu_s$. Given the states, sample the exterior regions of the connectors independently, each conditioned on its success $S_e$, and sample the remaining plane data ordinarily. The exterior region of $e$ may be the whole sector $\mathcal B_e$ with the closed endpoint state disks removed.
- **qualified pin** ([source](#stmt-pin-qualification)): Let $P$ be an oriented rim in a bulk patch. A qualified pin consists of a vertex $p\in P$, a grid center $z$ with $\|p-z\|\le h$, a placed menu type, its root ranks, and the following data. We describe the black case; interchanging colors gives the white case. 1. In the original configuration there are three surrounding gate circuits, in prescribed annuli $[a_kh,b_kh]$, $k=1,2,3$. The first two are the annuli of the plug. The third has $a_3>100b_2$ and isolates an ordered central subarc $\Delta$ through $p$: all visits of $P$ to $B(z,a_3h)$ belong to $\Delta$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\Delta\le2b_3h$. 2. In the nominal chart the local ordered traversal of $P$ follows the horizontal midline from before $u=-8$ to after $u=8$, with transverse error and longitudinal backtracking less than $0.1$. The arc $\Delta$ lies in $\|u\|<1$, and its marked point is near $u=0$. There are no other visits to $H$. The fit test has the closed-limit property of Lemma <a href="#lemma:exact-fit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:exact-fit">39</a> below. 3. A prescribed local preparation, applied to the original sample using an auxiliary seed attached to this chart, placement, and color, only recolors existing sites in the opposing strip from black to white, keeping the point configuration fixed. It leaves the fitted black traversal unchanged. Its inner state $\sigma'$ belongs to $G$, and the chosen type is admitted. The black root contains vertices of the *actual traversal* near $u=-1.7$ and $u=1.7$, with error less than $0.05$, joined along that traversal in the root window. The two black side passages are also supplied by that traversal. All these paths are retained paths. 4. For the white color separately, each of the two ordinary side-test probabilities, conditional on $\sigma'$ *and the actual point geometry*, is at least $\beta$. The prepared state is shared whenever the same candidate choice is used; preparations for other choices start again from the original sample.
- **real-arc mass convention** ([source](#sec:actual-arcs)): For two ordered pins $s,t$ on $P$, write $D_s=B_s=\overline B(z_s,m_sh)$ for the previously defined placed cutoff ball at $s$, and similarly $D_t=B_t$ at $t$. Define $$I_X(P;s,t) =X_P(\mathbf 1_{D_s}) +\sum_{\substack{e\text{ on the forward arc }p_s\to p_t\\ \text{right tag of }e\notin D_s\cup D_t}} X_P(e),\qquad X\in\{Q,V\}.$$
- **closing-star observable** ([source](#observable:definition)): At the closing star $t_*$, let $n_i$ be a point on the dual spoke crossing $s_{i-1}s_i$, and assign it the label $w_i=\omega^i$. This order is *opposite* to the positive cyclic order at $t_*$. For a probe $m$ in the interior of a dual edge, define, conditionally on the geometry $\eta$, $$F_m^\eta=4\sum_{i=0}^2w_iK(m,n_i) =4\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\bigl[w_*H_{\lambda_*}(m)\bigr]. \label{observable:definition}$$

          "exact_quote": "For each occupied circuit $L$, take the connected components $C$ of the topological graph $\\Gamma\\setminus L$: all points of $L$, including its vertices, are removed. In particular, an open edge whose two endpoints have been removed can be a component by itself. Figure <a href=\"#fig:component-labels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:component-labels\">1</a> illustrates the deletion convention in the tetrahedral dual. Put $\\lambda=(L,C)$ and, for a point $m$ in the interior of a dual edge, define $$H_\\lambda(m)=\\mathbf 1_{\\{m\\in C\\}}.$$ Vectors indexed by these pairs, with the index set taken separately in each configuration, have the scalar product $$\\langle F,G\\rangle\n   =\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{sp}}\\sum_\\lambda F_\\lambda\\overline{G_\\lambda}.$$ Here and throughout the finite discussion, $\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{sp}}$ averages colors with the triangulation fixed. We describe the black case; interchanging colors gives the white case.\n\n1.  In the original configuration there are three surrounding gate circuits, in prescribed annuli $[a_kh,b_kh]$, $k=1,2,3$. The first two are the annuli of the plug. The third has $a_3>100b_2$ and isolates an ordered central subarc $\\Delta$ through $p$: all visits of $P$ to $B(z,a_3h)$ belong to $\\Delta$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\Delta\\le2b_3h$.\n\n2.  In the nominal chart the local ordered traversal of $P$ follows the horizontal midline from before $u=-8$ to after $u=8$, with transverse error and longitudinal backtracking less than $0.1$. The arc $\\Delta$ lies in $|u|<1$, and its marked point is near $u=0$. There are no other visits to $H$. The fit test has the closed-limit property of Lemma <a href=\"#lemma:exact-fit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:exact-fit\">39</a> below.\n\n3.  A prescribed local preparation, applied to the original sample using an auxiliary seed attached to this chart, placement, and color, only recolors existing sites in the opposing strip from black to white, keeping the point configuration fixed. It leaves the fitted black traversal unchanged. Its inner state $\\sigma'$ belongs to $G$, and the chosen type is admitted. The black root contains vertices of the *actual traversal* near $u=-1.7$ and $u=1.7$, with error less than $0.05$, joined along that traversal in the root window. The two black side passages are also supplied by that traversal. All these paths are retained paths.\n\n4.  For the white color separately, each of the two ordinary side-test probabilities, conditional on $\\sigma'$ *and the actual point geometry*, is at least $\\beta$.\n\nThe prepared state is shared whenever the same candidate choice is used; preparations for other choices start again from the original sample.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-poisson-intensity | Poisson point intensity | = | $\varepsilon^{-2}$ | positive microscopic spacing |
| q-fair-site-color | probability of either site color | = | $1/2$ | all sites, conditional on the point process |
| q-field-cross-inner-product | \langle Z_t,Z_u\rangle | = | $0$ | distinct triangle stars |
| q-field-diagonal-norm | \\|Z_t\\|^2 | = | $\frac34$ | triangle star |
| q-affine-area-imbalance | \|p_t\|^2-\|q_t\|^2 | = | $\frac4{\sqrt3}\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(t)$ | clockwise side decomposition |
| q-antilinear-bessel-energy | \mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\sum_\lambda \|Q_\lambda(g)\|^2 | = | $\frac34\sum_t\|g(z_t)q_t\|^2$ | geometry-dependent color-independent weights |
| q-linear-bessel-energy | \mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\sum_\lambda \|U_\lambda(g)\|^2 | = | $\frac34\sum_t\|g(z_t)p_t\|^2$ | geometry-dependent color-independent weights |
| q-tangent-bessel-bound | \mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\sum_\lambda \|V_\lambda(g)\|^2 | ≤ | $\frac32\sum_t\|g(z_t)\|^2\bigl(\|p_t\|^2+\|q_t\|^2\bigr).$$$ | all labels and color-independent weights |
| q-quenched-arm-second-moment | \mathbb E\bigl[p_j^\eta(r,R)^2\bigr] | ≤ | $C_j\alpha_j(r,R)^2$ | fixed arm count and radii above the unit cutoff |
| q-one-arm-decay | \alpha_1(r,R) | ≤ | $C(r/R)^{c_1}$ | positive unspecified exponent and finite unspecified prefactor |
| q-five-arm-decay | \alpha_5(r,R) | ≤ | $C(r/R)^2$ | radii above the unit cutoff |
| q-four-arm-decay | \alpha_4(r,R) | ≤ | $C(r/R)^{1+c_4}$ | positive unspecified exponent improvement |
| q-disjoint-witness-product | \mathbb P(A\mathbin\square B) | ≤ | $\mathbb P(A)\mathbb P(B)$ | disjoint certifying coordinate sets; percolation use conditional on geometry |
| q-triangle-diameter-moments | \left\\|\sum_{t\cap S\ne\varnothing}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k\right\\|_{L^p} | ≤ | $C_{p,k}\varepsilon^{k-2}(s+\varepsilon)^2$ | square size, microscopic spacing, polynomial degree and moment order |
| q-barycenter-weight-intensity | \mathbb E\sum_{z_t\in E}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(t)^k | = | $m_k\varepsilon^{k-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(E)$ | polynomial diameter degree and finite-area deterministic mask |
| q-mixed-four-arm-upper-limit | limiting upper probability of mixed-color disjoint arms | ≤ | $C(s/S)^{1+c}$ | fixed center and fixed physical inner and outer radii |
| q-near-return-upper-limit | limiting upper probability of separated rim returns | ≤ | $C(s/S)^{2+c}$ | fixed deterministic center and physical radii |
| q-connector-success-lower-bound | \nu(S_e\mid\sigma_s,\sigma_t) | ≥ | $c_e$ | fixed connector and admitted endpoint types; no uniformity over arbitrary thin fitted patterns |
| q-short-cochain-norm | \\|i_e^\varepsilon\\|_{L^2(\mu_s\otimes\mu_t)} | ≤ | $C h$ | fixed normalized short-template family |
| q-tangent-scalar | a_h^V | = | $1$ | comparison scales in the common extraction |
| q-standard-route-separation | \|z_t-z_s\| | > | $Ch(1+k)$ | fixed types, comparison scale and twist integers |
| q-departure-potential-norm | \\|\phi_s^{X,+}\\|_{L^2(\mu_s)} | ≤ | $C_{\rm short}h$ | fixed normalized menu and short templates |
| q-arrival-potential-norm | \\|\phi_t^{X,-}\\|_{L^2(\mu_t)} | ≤ | $C_{\rm short}h$ | fixed normalized menu and short templates |
| q-endpoint-bias-bound | \limsup_{\varepsilon\to0}\|b_e^\varepsilon\| | ≤ | $C_{\rm short}h(1+k)$ | fixed pattern before microscopic limit; common short-template constant |
| q-retained-circumradius | \rho_\varepsilon | = | $\sqrt{h\varepsilon}$ | fixed comparison scale; triangles of circumradius at most the threshold |
| q-real-arc-error | real-arc error after affine displacement and endpoint potentials | ≤ | $C_Rh(1+\|\log h\|^2)$ | microscopic limit before decreasing comparison scale |
| q-mixed-atom-exponent | \kappa | < | $c/2$ | positive exponent chosen below the common arm exponent threshold |
| q-nondegenerate-area-coefficient | 1-2\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}a | > | $0$ | every scalar produced by the stated construction |
| q-rectangle-quenched-variance | \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}(Q_\varepsilon) | ≤ | $C_\lambda(\varepsilon/h)^\alpha$ | fixed rectangle aspect ratio and geometric half-height |
