<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-two-defect-translation** [claim](#stmt-fields-defects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-defects): Toggle a path between prescribed defects to biject even and defect ensembles while preserving a fixed occupied circuit. -> circuit-count representation of the kernel
- **step-star-fourier-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-fields-orthogonality-prop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-orthogonality-prop): Delete local stars, classify exterior port pairings, and cancel their Fourier-weighted circuit counts. -> exact off-diagonal orthogonality and diagonal norm
- **step-component-boundary-rims** [claim](#stmt-lemma-rim) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-rim): Use planar alternating-contact separation to make component sectors contiguous and their primal boundary simple. -> unique rim, right tags, and isolated-edge cancellation
- **step-rim-extremality** [claim](#stmt-lemma-no-bypass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-no-bypass): An alleged right bypass separates facing vertices of the same opposing cluster; the converse compares the candidate to its component rim. -> no-right-bypass characterization
- **step-annular-rim-sandwich** [claim](#stmt-lemma-sandwich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-sandwich): Take the face boundary of a maximal black biconnected block toward the white circuit and apply rim extremality. -> confined oriented black rim
- **step-critical-input-contract** [claim](#stmt-prop-probability-input) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Import the cited full-plane crossing and arm estimates at their stated fixed-shape and unit-cutoff hypotheses. -> crossing lower bounds, arm comparisons, and power bounds
- **step-fair-bit-disjoint-product** [claim](#stmt-prob-reimer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-reimer): Apply the fair-bit disjoint-occurrence inequality on agreement slices of configuration pairs and sum the slice bounds. -> conditional product probability inequality
- **step-poisson-local-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lemma-mesh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-mesh): Use empty-disk tests, Poisson count moments, stationarity, ergodicity, and winding extraction of local cycles. -> local determination, geometric tails, moments, and ordered cycles
- **step-stationary-closed-rim-tail** [claim](#stmt-fields-closed-prop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-closed-prop): Bound stationary anchored squared mass by field energy, pass to complete rims, then rescale the large-diameter tail. -> vanishing macroscopic closed antilinear sums
- **step-hex-indicator-duality** [claim](#stmt-prob-hex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-hex): Attach alternating exterior fans and read noncrossing strand pairings; recolor a blocking set by its indicator. -> opposing crossing alternative and transverse blocking-set path
- **step-leftmost-color-peeling** [claim](#stmt-prob-color-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-color-order): Expose the leftmost path and induct on the free fair colors strictly to its right. -> color-sequence independence for disjoint crossings
- **step-mixed-arm-strand-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-mixed-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-mixed-four): Slit along each fixed opposite-color wall strand, use ordered-color invariance, and control strand multiplicity by conditional crossing tails. -> mixed-color arm decay
- **step-near-return-arm-extraction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-near-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-near-return): Use rim extremality and indicator Hex crossings to extract disjoint opposing arms, then combine conditional disjoint occurrence and arm second moments. -> local near-return probability bound
- **step-menger-two-site-gates** [claim](#stmt-lemma-two-gates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-two-gates): Recolor a small black vertex cut white, separate capped boundary components by a wall, and extract an essential white circuit before restoring colors. -> essential circuit with controlled black contacts
- **step-buffered-conditional-bridges** [claim](#stmt-lemma-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-bridge): Concatenate fixed buffered crossing networks using color-specific product orders, then integrate geometry before subtracting its exceptional event. -> positive conditional connector success
- **step-cycle-confined-rim** [claim](#stmt-lemma-cycle-rim) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-cycle-rim): Concatenate successful root passages into opposing essential circuits and sandwich a rim; gate contact counts force the ordered passages. -> confined rim with ordered gate contacts
- **step-two-graph-gate-agreement** [claim](#stmt-lemma-gate-consistency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-gate-consistency): At the first divergence, planar ray order puts a mutually present monochromatic path into the other rim's right side; its first whole-rim return is forbidden. -> exact directed path identity
- **step-deterministic-path-collars** [claim](#stmt-lemma-fixed-collar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-fixed-collar): Inset band layers and shielded state cuts give fixed compact envelopes; retained circumdisks preserve all incident triangles. -> common incident triangulations in shared data
- **step-state-cochain-resampling** [claim](#stmt-prop-state-cochain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-state-cochain): Compare shared-state completions, condition cutoff and sector masses, then independently resample a sector and subtract closed cycle sums. -> completion-independent state cochain with small cycle sums
- **step-common-complement-subtraction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-common-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-common-closure): Subtract the shared complementary cochain from both simple-system cycle sums. -> asymptotic path comparison on the identified state product
- **step-full-port-band-construction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-band-collar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-band-collar): Taper prescribed end collars, straighten the middle arc, construct separated boundary sides, and fill the resulting Jordan rectangles. -> buffered attached band avoiding specified compact routes
- **step-buffered-chart-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lemma-chart-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-chart-stability): Compare mixed and pure cycle experiments through retained nominal sectors, identify their gate paths, and subtract the common complementary contribution. -> asymptotic chart stability
- **step-straight-mean-additivity** [claim](#stmt-prop-affine) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-affine): Insert intermediate reference plugs, extract countably many mean limits, and extend bounded additivity to a continuous linear displacement rule. -> bounded affine scalars for straight means
- **step-tangent-contour-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lemma-tangent-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-tangent-normalization): Compare barycenter masks with displacement integrals on elongated polygonal sectors and bound fixed end corrections independently of the straight gap length. -> exact tangent scalar normalization
- **step-endpoint-twist-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-two-twists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-two-twists): Normalize obstacles to round holes, smooth and radialize arcs, compensate moving endpoint angles in disjoint collars, and detect the remaining twists by net arguments. -> endpoint-twist classification and argument control
- **step-standard-spiral-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lemma-standard-spirals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-potentials-2): Build separated opposite polygonal spirals, isolate bounded corner templates with reference copies, and join unused strands by a remote common complement. -> standard representative with uniformly controlled short pieces
- **step-endpoint-potential-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint-potentials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-potentials): Compare to the standard inserted representative, average auxiliary states, center the fixed endpoint insertions, and assign only short-piece mean deviations to the deterministic bias. -> local potentials, twist-controlled bias, and vanishing remainder
- **step-inverse-diameter-profile-limits** [claim](#stmt-lemma-profile-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-profile-compactness): Construct finite marked Fréchet nets using displacement stops, align a Cauchy subsequence, collapse constant fibers, and pass complementary sets to Hausdorff limits. -> compact family of simple separated limiting profiles
- **step-exact-ambient-profile-fit** [claim](#stmt-lemma-exact-fit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-exact-fit): Straighten the ordered arc by prescribed-boundary topology, construct nested slab-compatible disks, and extend close crosscut correspondences through conformal charts before taking a finite cover. -> finite plug menu with exact closed-limit fitting
- **step-local-preparations-dense-pins** [claim](#stmt-prop-dense-pins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dense-pins): Combine independent annular trials, finite profile fits, monotone local preparations, root-rank truncation, and propensity thresholds; exclude long chains of bad blocks. -> simultaneous dense qualified pins in the ordered limit
- **step-dominated-forcing-measures** [claim](#stmt-lemma-dominated-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-dominated-kernel): Disintegrate the ordinary measure on retained-data fibers and use kernel positivity; under the extra product-spin hypothesis apply a finite monotone coupling while fixing complementary coordinates. -> domination after forcing without fit normalization
- **step-retained-sector-locality** [claim](#stmt-lemma-retained-collar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-retained-collar): Keep open collars containing every retained circumdisk needed by the inset path, states, and right tags. -> identical local triangulations despite complementary resampling
- **step-net-winding-tail** [claim](#stmt-lemma-winding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-winding): Bin ray passages by radial scale, extract disjoint annular crossings from successive lifted argument turns, and apply conditional crossing tails over the fixed grid family. -> simultaneous net-argument control
- **step-real-arc-cycle-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-real-arc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-real-arc): Prepare assigned endpoints, force only white exterior spins, complete while retaining the used sector, identify every fitting input arc with one output rim, and project dominated errors back to the original input. -> uniform original-arc transport estimate
- **step-signed-mixed-atom-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lemma-mixed-atoms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-mixed-atoms): Use exact chunk concatenation and summation by parts, then stop unmixed deterministic spatial atoms and refine mixed ones using near-return probabilities and full-label energy. -> uniform signed-field coefficient replacement
- **step-common-bulk-scalar** [claim](#stmt-thm-bulk-transport) / [proof](#pm-h-50): Combine endpoint-potential cancellation and mixed-atom replacement, extract a bounded comparison-scale scalar limit, and use the tangent energy bound to replace the intermediate scalars. -> universal bulk transport residual convergence
- **step-area-pairing-nondegeneracy** [claim](#stmt-prop-nondegeneracy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nondegeneracy): Replace tangent fields by contour integrals, control small rims and macroscopic wall multiplicity, prove pairing uniform integrability, and compare the exact energy difference with positive area. -> strict scalar nondegeneracy
- **step-polygonal-primal-disk-approximation** [claim](#stmt-lemma-polygonal-disks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-polygonal-disks): Trace nearby cell sites in boundary order, decompose the resulting walk into simple cycles, retain positive winding, and use Jordan injectivity to control dropped intervals. -> geometry-selected ordered discrete disks
- **step-closing-star-crossing-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-prop-observable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-observable): Move star defects to ports, read the observable as a mean partner label, and split a fan into alternating exterior colors without fixing original disk spins. -> triangular positivity, side constraints, and exact crossing coefficient
- **step-annealed-probe-limit** [claim](#stmt-lemma-probe-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-probe-compactness): Control all nearby geometry-selected probe differences by one ordinary conditional arm test, then diagonally extract dense representatives and extend continuously. -> continuous annealed subsequential observable
- **step-correlated-edge-measure-holomorphy** [claim](#stmt-prop-holomorphy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-holomorphy): Use closing-star orthogonality and bulk transport, group shared-edge coefficients into a random measure, prove its deterministic limit on fixed squares, and retain correlations until the continuous weak equation is obtained. -> holomorphic limit observable
- **step-boundary-arc-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-lemma-boundary-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-boundary-uniqueness): Reflect on open arcs, obtain monotone argument lifts, rule out jumps by boundedness of the Cauchy integral, extend through the Poisson formula, and apply the argument principle. -> continuous marked conformal bijection
- **step-jordan-fixed-buffer-squeeze** [claim](#stmt-prop-jordan-squeeze) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-jordan-squeeze): Use a prescribed-boundary square chart, fix harder and easier polygonal comparison disks, convert crossings through cell contacts and primal chains, then shrink buffers after the microscopic limit. -> exact annealed crossing law on arbitrary Jordan domains
- **step-cardy-function-identification** [claim](#stmt-thm-cardy) / [proof](#pm-h-64): Identify the boundary coefficient through the conformal triangle map and substitute the beta and hypergeometric integral identities into the Jordan crossing limit. -> the stated annealed Cardy formula
- **step-rectangle-quenched-concentration** [claim](#stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles): Identify the rectangle's connected-set and continuous crossing events, apply the scaled conditional variance estimate, and combine variance with the annealed mean limit. -> quenched mean-square and probability convergence

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-hex-crossing**: In a finite triangulated disk with successive boundary sides, a black crossing of one opposite pair or a white crossing of the other pair exists.
- **invariant-blocking-set-crossing**: A vertex set blocking every path between opposite sides contains a path between the other opposite sides.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-scaling-proof**: Treat exhaustive checks of finite disk crossings as certification of the annealed Cardy limit. -> Finite color enumeration does not verify the microscopic limit, the Poisson geometry law, or the conformal identification.
- **anti-delete-boundary-contacts**: Remove corner vertices from the boundary sides when checking discrete crossings. -> The modified event can lose the connections asserted for inclusive vertex sides.
- **anti-arbitrary-planar-graph-hex**: Apply the Hex disjunction to an arbitrary planar graph with missing triangulation edges. -> The disk separation argument requires triangulation; planarity alone does not supply the asserted primal crossing.
- **anti-annealed-disjoint-product**: Multiply annealed event probabilities when combining disjoint crossing witnesses. -> The product inequality is conditional on fixed geometry; averaging produces an expectation of a product of conditional probabilities.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite constructive target is the Hex and indicator-coloring lemma, not the asymptotic crossing theorem. Its proof states: "For the last assertion, color *exactly* the vertices of $X$ black and every other vertex white." The implementation enumerates all colorings of explicitly triangulated disk fixtures and returns crossing witnesses. Coverage remains partial for the unrestricted family of finite triangulated disks.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with fixture equal to diagonal, wheel-square, or wheel-hexagon.
# PRECONDITIONS: The named fixture is a fixed triangulated disk; boundary sides include endpoints.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing a path or null for each of four crossing tests in every coloring.
# TIME: Exponential in fixture vertex count because every coloring is enumerated; each search is linear in graph size.
# SPACE: Linear search storage plus the complete enumerated output.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer bit masks, vertex indices, and Boolean connectivity.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; a violated Hex or blocking implication raises AssertionError.
import json
import sys
from collections import deque

request = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(request, dict) or set(request) != {'fixture'}:
    raise ValueError('invalid input contract')
fixture = request['fixture']
if fixture == 'diagonal':
    n = 4
    edges = [(0, 1), (1, 2), (2, 3), (3, 0), (0, 2)]
    sides = [[0, 1], [1, 2], [2, 3], [3, 0]]
elif fixture == 'wheel-square':
    n = 5
    edges = [(i, (i + 1) % 4) for i in range(4)] + [(i, 4) for i in range(4)]
    sides = [[0, 1], [1, 2], [2, 3], [3, 0]]
elif fixture == 'wheel-hexagon':
    n = 7
    edges = [(i, (i + 1) % 6) for i in range(6)] + [(i, 6) for i in range(6)]
    sides = [[0, 1], [1, 2, 3], [3, 4], [4, 5, 0]]
else:
    raise ValueError('unknown fixture')

adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
for u, v in edges:
    adj[u].append(v)
    adj[v].append(u)
for neighbors in adj:
    neighbors.sort()

records = []
for mask in range(1 << n):
    colors = [(mask >> v) & 1 for v in range(n)]
    def crossing(left, right, color):
        starts = [v for v in sides[left] if colors[v] == color]
        goals = {v for v in sides[right] if colors[v] == color}
        parents = {v: None for v in starts}
        queue = deque(starts)
        while queue:
            u = queue.popleft()
            if u in goals:
                path = []
                while u is not None:
                    path.append(u)
                    u = parents[u]
                return path[::-1]
            for v in adj[u]:
                if colors[v] == color and v not in parents:
                    parents[v] = u
                    queue.append(v)
        return None
    black_first = crossing(0, 2, 1)
    white_other = crossing(1, 3, 0)
    black_other = crossing(1, 3, 1)
    white_first = crossing(0, 2, 0)
    assert black_first is not None or white_other is not None, 'Hex violation'
    assert white_first is not None or black_other is not None, 'blocking-set violation'
    records.append([black_first, white_other, black_other, white_first])
json.dump({'records': records}, sys.stdout, separators=(',', ':'))
```

          "source_object": "stmt-lemma-two-gates"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lemma-two-gates",
          "exact_quote": "An occupied dual circuit separates the caps. To see this directly, sites lying in the same component off all disagreement walls are joined in their color: a curve in that component, made transverse to dual edges, crosses only unoccupied dual edges and hence only equal-colored primal adjacencies. Since the caps are not connected, at least one wall separates them. The neighboring white walk along that wall uses original annular sites and is deformable to the wall while avoiding the caps. It therefore has nonzero annular winding. Decompose the walk into simple cycles and retain one with nonzero winding; it is an essential white circuit. On restoring the original colors, only the cut vertices can become black, so the circuit has at most two black vertices.",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-lemma-two-gates"
        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-homogeneous-fair-planar-model** (hypothesis): The crossing law concerns homogeneous planar Poisson geometry and independent fair site colors. Biased coloring, spatially varying intensity, and nonplanar models require separate results. Blocked use: ['Applying the crossing formula to correlated colors or an inhomogeneous point process.', 'Transferring the planar crossing law to volumetric connectivity.']
- **limit-fixed-marked-jordan-domain** (hypothesis): The domain must be fixed, bounded, and Jordan, with distinct boundary marks in the prescribed cyclic order. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for self-intersecting boundaries or coincident marks.', 'Allowing the domain to vary with the microscopic spacing without an additional comparison theorem.']
- **limit-connected-closed-cell-event** (hypothesis): The target crossing is a connected subset of the closed black-cell union intersected with the closed domain. Boundary contacts, including contacts at cell vertices, belong to this convention. Blocked use: ['Replacing the event by an open-cell crossing that excludes boundary contacts.', 'Identifying an arbitrary clipped graph-path event with the target crossing without proving the necessary comparison.']
- **limit-annealed-scalar-scope** (formal_scope): The main result identifies a scalar probability limit averaged over geometry and colors. It does not establish a full interface distribution or an arbitrary-domain quenched limit. Blocked use: ['Claiming convergence of complete interface curves from the crossing formula.', 'Treating the formula as a deterministic guarantee for an individual geometry in a general Jordan domain.']
- **limit-rectangle-quenched-consequence** (hypothesis): The quenched consequence concerns fixed nondegenerate rectangles and convergence over the geometry law at each mesh. It supplies no almost-sure convergence assertion under a coupling across meshes. Blocked use: ['Extending the rectangle consequence to arbitrary Jordan domains.', 'Claiming almost-sure convergence for a chosen sequence of coupled geometries.']
- **limit-no-general-finite-mesh-certificate** (nonconstructive): The main crossing theorem is a microscopic limit without a stated finite-spacing error bound. Finite sample agreement with its limit is not a certificate of the theorem or of simulation accuracy. Blocked use: ['Deriving a finite-mesh acceptance tolerance solely from the limiting crossing formula.', 'Certifying the continuum result through exhaustive checks on finite graph fixtures.']
- **limit-complete-component-labels** (hypothesis): The exact field identities retain actual complement components after deletion of every circuit point, including isolated open edges. Restriction to genuine rims yields inequalities rather than the complete energy equalities. Blocked use: ['Merging all components on the same geometric side of a wall.', 'Dropping isolated open-edge components from an exact energy calculation.']
- **limit-color-independent-energy-weights** (hypothesis): The conditional energy bounds allow geometry-dependent coefficients but require independence from the colors. Adaptive color-selected coefficients need the separate deterministic refinement argument. Blocked use: ['Applying the conditional Bessel bounds directly to masks selected from a colored rim.', 'Using small pointwise coefficient errors as sufficient control of a signed field.']
- **limit-complete-rims-before-opening** (hypothesis): The stationary closed-sum estimate applies to complete macroscopic rims. Open portions require the subsequent endpoint-state and closure comparisons. Blocked use: ['Applying the closed-rim tail directly to an arbitrary proper subarc.']
- **limit-full-plane-crossing-input** (hypothesis): The imported crossing estimates use the ordinary full-plane law and fixed buffered shape families. They do not provide lower bounds under arbitrary conditioning of the point process. Blocked use: ['Using ordinary crossing lower bounds after arbitrary adaptive geometry conditioning.', 'Replacing fixed buffered rectangle families by unrestricted random shapes.']
- **limit-fixed-geometry-disjoint-product** (hypothesis): Disjoint occurrence is bounded in a finite fair-bit product conditional on the geometry. Averaging requires control of the product of conditional probabilities. Blocked use: ['Multiplying annealed event probabilities to bound disjoint occurrence.', 'Applying the fair-bit inequality to dependent exterior colors.']
- **limit-fixed-arm-centers-and-scales** (hypothesis): The local mixed-arm and near-return estimates use deterministic centers and physical radii fixed before the microscopic limit. Adaptive centers and arbitrary microscopic inner radii are outside the stated regime. Blocked use: ['Applying the local bound at a center selected from the colors without further justification.', 'Substituting a spacing-dependent inner radius into a fixed-scale limiting estimate.']
- **limit-ordered-comparison-limits** (hypothesis): Comparison scale, deterministic patterns, and refinement depth remain fixed during the microscopic limit. Refinement is subsequently increased before the comparison scale decreases. Blocked use: ['Taking a simultaneous microscopic and comparison-scale limit without uniform estimates.', 'Letting the comparison family grow during a limit proved only for fixed finite families.']
- **limit-admission-and-side-propensities** (hypothesis): Connector comparisons require admitted usable states. Ordinary conditional side probabilities average exterior geometry and colors; the later white forcing additionally requires side probabilities conditional on the actual geometry. Blocked use: ['Inferring a quenched side lower bound from an annealed propensity.', 'Using a nonadmitted endpoint type in the conditioned cycle construction.']
- **limit-simple-systems-and-mutual-path-presence** (hypothesis): Cycle experiments require embedded simple systems with separated obstacles and exterior bands. Exact gate comparison requires each complete compared monochromatic subpath to be present in the other graph with the same embedding and compatible gate fans. Blocked use: ['Comparing paths using only common endpoint coordinates.', 'Replacing complete mutual path retention by agreement of a selected witness or a few edges.']
- **limit-buffered-chart-uniformity** (hypothesis): Chart stability is proved on buffered local templates with fixed stubs and no remote returns to moved neighborhoods. Thin fitted connectors do not supply the uniform short-template moment constants. Blocked use: ["Treating a thin fitted connector's conditioning constant as a uniform bound for all templates.", 'Moving endpoint charts when remote route pieces return to the moved neighborhoods.']
- **limit-endpoint-twists-and-separation** (hypothesis): The standard representative and endpoint expansion require terminal separation relative to comparison scale and twist size. The bias is controlled by short pieces; no linear bound on total route arclength follows. Blocked use: ['Applying the endpoint expansion when terminal separation fails.', 'Charging a length-proportional bias to all straight legs.', 'Interpreting the count of short pieces as a linear total-arclength estimate.']
- **limit-existential-profile-menu-and-scalar** (nonconstructive): Subsequence extraction, profile compactness, ambient extension, and finite covering establish existence. The paper does not specify an executable representation of an arbitrary limiting profile or identify the bulk scalar numerically. Blocked use: ['Claiming a finite algorithm for the universal plug menu directly from compactness.', 'Assigning an explicit value to the extracted bulk scalar.']
- **limit-unnormalized-dominated-forcing** (hypothesis): Selected inputs remain unnormalized dominated subprobabilities. The forcing event must have a positive ordinary conditional lower bound; monotone recoloring additionally requires the specified finite product-spin block and increasing event. Blocked use: ['Dividing by an adaptive fitting probability and claiming unchanged domination constants.', 'Forcing an arbitrary nonmonotone event while preserving the stated white-adding kernel.', 'Assuming selected exterior spins retain their ordinary conditional product law.']
- **limit-retained-circumdisks-and-open-collars** (hypothesis): Local triangulation agreement uses kept open collars and triangles whose circumradii are at most the retention threshold. Keeping path vertices alone does not determine the required empty-circumdisk and incident-tag data. Blocked use: ['Claiming locality for triangles above the retained circumradius threshold.', 'Resampling points inside an incident circumdisk while asserting that the compared tag remains unchanged.']
- **limit-qualified-real-arc-and-cutoff-convention** (hypothesis): Real-arc transport requires qualified ordered pins, the stated macroscopic endpoint separation and complementary excursion, and their already assigned prepared states. Exact concatenation includes the entire starting cutoff mass and omits the terminal cutoff. Blocked use: ['Applying the real-arc estimate to arbitrary unqualified endpoints.', 'Replacing assigned endpoint states during the comparison.', 'Counting only the forward part of the starting cutoff and still asserting exact concatenation.']
- **limit-pairing-expectations-require-integrability** (hypothesis): Bulk transport in probability does not alone justify summing over labels or passing to expectation. Contour replacement, small-rim control, wall multiplicity, and uniform integrability supply the additional justification. Blocked use: ['Passing a maximum-in-probability transport estimate directly into an unrestricted expected label pairing.', 'Replacing area multiplicity by an asserted bound on the total number of rims associated with one wall.']
- **limit-geometry-selected-probes-and-correlations** (hypothesis): Probe compactness concerns geometry-measurable admissible probes approaching fixed points away from the marks. Geometry-dependent edge weights remain correlated with conditional observables until their deterministic measure limit is proved. Blocked use: ['Selecting probes from colors and applying the geometry-measurable compactness assertion.', 'Replacing an expectation of geometry-dependent products by a product of expectations.']
- **limit-boundary-uniqueness-contract** (hypothesis): Boundary uniqueness requires a nonconstant holomorphic disk map, distinct positively ordered source and target marks, and continuous open-arc values in the corresponding closed target arcs. Blocked use: ['Applying uniqueness to a constant map.', 'Using unordered boundary correspondences or dropping the open-arc continuity assumption.']
- **limit-jordan-buffers-before-microscopic-limit** (hypothesis): The Jordan comparison fixes coordinate buffers and deterministic polygonal approximations during the microscopic limit, then shrinks the buffers. Uniform continuity suffices; no metric regularity of the physical boundary is assumed. Blocked use: ['Shrinking comparison buffers with the microscopic spacing without additional uniform control.', 'Assuming a Lipschitz chart or convex physical domain as part of the proved comparison.']
- **limit-finite-hex-verification-scope** (formal_scope): Finite constructive verification can check the discrete crossing alternative on the supplied triangulated disks. Such fixture exhaustion does not decide the unrestricted family of triangulated disks or the continuum crossing limit. Blocked use: ['Promoting partial fixture coverage to a proof of the general finite separation lemma.', 'Promoting finite connectivity checks to certification of the annealed scaling law.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Cardy's formula for annealed crossing probabilities in critical planar Poisson–Voronoi percolation in every bounded Jordan quadrilateral. This proves the annealed crossing-probability form of the conformal-invariance conjecture for this model.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Cardy’s formula predicts that a critical two-dimensional percolation crossing probability is determined by a conformal parameter of the marked domain (Cardy 1992) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Cardy1992](#ref-Cardy1992)]. Smirnov proved the formula for site percolation on the triangular lattice (Smirnov 2001b) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Smirnov2001](#ref-Smirnov2001)]. Schramm’s Problem 2.12 asks for the corresponding conformal-invariance theorem for Voronoi percolation (Schramm 2007) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Schramm2006](#ref-Schramm2006)]. We prove the annealed Cardy-crossing aspect of this conjecture for the Poisson model, with no regularity assumption on the boundary beyond the Jordan property.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The crossing law

Identify the oriented plane with $\mathbb C$. For $\varepsilon>0$, let $\eta=X_\varepsilon$ be a homogeneous Poisson point process of intensity $\varepsilon^{-2}$. For $v\in\eta$ its Voronoi cell is the closed set $$\mathcal V_v=\{z\in\mathbb C:|z-v|\le |z-u|\text{ for every }u\in\eta\}.$$ Independently of $\eta$, color its sites black or white with probability $1/2$ each, and give each cell the color of its site. Let $\mathcal B_\varepsilon$ be the union of the closed black cells. Probabilities and expectations under the joint law of the points and colors are denoted by $\mathbb P$ and $\mathbb E$. Conditional probabilities given the points are denoted by $\mathbb P^{\eta}$; $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}$ denotes averaging over independent fair colors on a fixed graph. Thus all probabilities in the main theorem are annealed.

Let $D$ be a bounded Jordan domain and let $a,b,c,d$ be distinct points of $\partial D$ in counterclockwise order. Boundary arcs always include their endpoints. Define $\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)$ to be the event that $\mathcal B_\varepsilon\cap\overline D$ contains a connected subset meeting both the arc $ab$ and the arc $cd$. The conformal map from $D$ to $\mathbb H=\{z:\operatorname{Im}z>0\}$ which sends $a,c,d$ to $0,1,\infty$ extends to the boundary in the sense of Carathéodory (Pommerenke 1992, vol. 299, sec. 2.1, p. 18) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Pommerenke1992](#ref-Pommerenke1992)]. Its value at $b$ is a uniquely determined number $x\in(0,1)$.

<a id="stmt-thm-cardy"></a>
<div id="thm:cardy" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:cardy"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Annealed Voronoi crossing law). *For every bounded Jordan domain $D$ and every four distinct counterclockwise boundary marks $a,b,c,d$, one has $$\label{eq:cardy}
 \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)\bigr)
 =F(x)
 :=\frac{\displaystyle\int_0^x[u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,\mathrm du}
          {\displaystyle\int_0^1[u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,\mathrm du}
 =\frac{\Gamma(2/3)x^{1/3}}
        {\Gamma(1/3)\Gamma(4/3)}
        {}_2F_1\!\left(\frac13,\frac23;\frac43;x\right).$$*

</div>

The theorem resolves positively the annealed Cardy-crossing conjecture for critical Poisson–Voronoi percolation, the crossing-probability aspect of Schramm’s Problem 2.12 above. Its scope is a limit of crossing probabilities averaged over geometry and colors. The statement does not assert convergence of the full interface law or a quenched crossing limit for arbitrary Jordan domains. In particular, the result concerns the specified closed-cell crossing event in every fixed Jordan domain, including domains for which a smooth boundary approximation alone would not immediately identify that event.

For a fixed rectangle, the established concentration of conditional crossing probabilities also gives a quenched $L^2$ limit; see Corollary <a href="#cor:quenched-rectangles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:quenched-rectangles">2</a> below.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Prior work

The conformal-invariance conjecture for critical crossings developed from numerical and universality investigations. Langlands, Pouliot and Saint-Aubin explicitly credit Aizenman with the conformal formulation (Langlands, Pouliot, and Saint-Aubin 1994, sec. 2.1 and 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LanglandsPouliotSaintAubin1994](#ref-LanglandsPouliotSaintAubin1994)]. Cardy’s conformal-field-theory calculation identifies the predicted crossing function (Cardy 1992) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Cardy1992](#ref-Cardy1992)]. Smirnov’s proof on the triangular lattice uses color-switching identities to construct conformal observables (Smirnov 2001b, 2001a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Smirnov2001](#ref-Smirnov2001), [Smirnov2001Expanded](#ref-Smirnov2001Expanded)]. Carleson’s representation of Cardy’s formula as a linear boundary value in an equilateral triangle, explained in (Smirnov 2001a, Corollary 3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Smirnov2001Expanded](#ref-Smirnov2001Expanded)], also describes the boundary problem at the end of our argument.

The same Cardy function is obtained for critical square-lattice bond percolation under uniform marked-Jordan approximation (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAISquareBond2026](#ref-OpenAISquareBond2026)]. There the event is an actual open-edge path with all boundary edges sampled independently and no external connections; here it is an annealed closed-cell crossing in a fixed Jordan domain. The square-bond theorem is not used in our proof.

Voronoi percolation provides a random planar geometry whose law has no preferred lattice direction. Benjamini and Schramm proved a distinct high-intensity invariance under smooth conformal changes of metric, with the sampling measure held fixed (Benjamini and Schramm 1998) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BenjaminiSchramm1998](#ref-BenjaminiSchramm1998)]. Bollobás and Riordan established that the critical probability of the planar Poisson model is $1/2$ (Bollobás and Riordan 2006) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BollobasRiordan2006](#ref-BollobasRiordan2006)]. Uniform box-crossing estimates were established by Tassion (Tassion 2016) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Tassion2016](#ref-Tassion2016)]; quenched crossing estimates were developed by Ahlberg, Griffiths, Morris and Tassion (Ahlberg et al. 2016) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AGMT2016](#ref-AGMT2016)]. Vanneuville’s quantitative work supplies comparisons between quenched and annealed arm probabilities, as well as the crossing estimates used here (Vanneuville 2025) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Vanneuville2025](#ref-Vanneuville2025)]. The conformal-invariance conjecture and its relation to these partial results are discussed in (Vanneuville 2021, sec. 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Vanneuville2021](#ref-Vanneuville2021)]. These estimates control crossings and exceptional geometry; identifying the limiting function requires an additional argument. Proposition <a href="#prop:probability-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:probability-input">10</a> states the established estimates at the precise hypotheses used below.

The challenge is to obtain an analytic relation that survives the irregular embedding. In a related setting, Beffara separates a color-switching identity from the geometric defect of a non-equilateral embedding (Beffara 2014, secs. 3.2–3.3, equations (13)–(14)) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Beffara2014Mesoscopic](#ref-Beffara2014Mesoscopic)]. His crossing theorem concerns periodic triangulations subdivided into triangular-lattice pieces, under uniform box-crossing and a specified relation between the two scales; its limit agrees with triangular-lattice percolation up to a real-linear transformation determined by the initial embedding. Here the geometry is Poisson–Delaunay; we recover the needed relation by transporting exact closed-circuit identities to open portions of random boundaries.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From circuit identities to the crossing law

The proof begins with the triangular graph joining sites of neighboring Voronoi cells and its dual graph. Dual edges separating opposite colors form ordinary cluster-boundary circuits. For each such circuit $L$, we delete all points of $L$, including its vertices, and retain each connected component $C$ of the remaining dual graph. An unused edge whose two endpoints lie on $L$ then leaves an isolated open-edge component. The label is the pair $(L,C)$, not just the geometric side of $L$. Except for isolated open-edge components, these components have simple monochromatic primal boundaries $P$, called *rims*, oriented with their component on the left. Keeping the full component label, including isolated edges, is what makes the exact orthogonality identity of Section <a href="#sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fields">2</a> hold.

Each triangle is the image of a reference equilateral triangle under an orientation-preserving real-affine map. The complex-linear and antilinear parts of this map split each clockwise side vector into two terms. For a smooth test function $f$, take the contributions for the side belonging to $P$ in each triangle immediately to its right, and weight them by $f$ at the triangle’s barycenter. Summing the complex-linear terms gives $U_P(f)$; summing the antilinear terms gives $Q_P(f)$. Thus $Q$ records the departure from equilateral geometry, while $V_P(f)=U_P(f)+Q_P(f)$ is the weighted tangent displacement. Orthogonality gives a separate exact second-moment identity for each Fourier field when summed over all component labels, including isolated open edges. The complete tangent sum is $V_P(1)=0$ on every closed rim. Stationarity makes the corresponding macroscopic closed-rim sums of $Q$ small. The main task is to open that identity: along a common microscopic subsequence, we prove $$Q_P(f)-aV_P(f)\longrightarrow0$$ in probability, uniformly over rims extending beyond a fixed collar of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}f$. The scalar $a$ is independent of the later test and bulk patch; Theorem <a href="#thm:bulk-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bulk-transport">18</a> states the precise local formulation.

For this passage to open arcs, we retain local colored configurations near endpoints in deterministic regions called *plugs*. Separated two-color corridors join them into closed experiments. Surrounding circuits restrict the path to the same two contacts, and the absence of an alternative monochromatic path on its right forces agreement when the exterior configuration is changed. For each fixed connector, conditional resampling produces a function of the two endpoint states, common to its completions, whose sum around each fixed cycle is small. Comparing closures and inserting intermediate plugs decomposes this function into an affine displacement, two local endpoint potentials, and a controlled remainder. Sections <a href="#sec:plugs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:plugs">4</a> and <a href="#sec:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine">5</a> construct this endpoint transport before it is used on a random arc.

Two issues remain in returning to actual rims. The local geometry and colors have already selected the arc, so the comparison must retain their conditioning. At each fixed comparison scale, arm estimates provide a finite menu of plug models densely along every relevant rim and a finite deterministic family of connecting patterns. The comparison is made with unnormalized subprobabilities dominated by the original law; there is no division by a configuration-dependent fitting probability. A second issue is cancellation: the fields are signed, so a small change in their coefficients is not controlled by a path-length estimate. Deterministic spatial refinement, a near-return bound, and the orthogonality identity supply that control. Sections <a href="#sec:pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pins">6</a> and <a href="#sec:actual-arcs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:actual-arcs">7</a> complete these two steps in the order needed for bulk transport.

The final steps prove $1-2\operatorname{Re}a>0$ for the scalar $a$ appearing in the transport relation. In particular, the coefficient $1-a$ needed by the observable is nonzero. Complete a finite discrete disk by three exterior marked vertices; a component observable at this closing triangle records which boundary connection occurs. One of its boundary coefficients is exactly the crossing probability. Orthogonality and the transport relation give the contour cancellation needed for a holomorphic limit. The scalar $a$ need not be identified: once $1-a\ne0$, it disappears from the limiting Cauchy–Riemann equation. The resulting boundary-value problem has the conformal map to an equilateral triangle as its unique solution (Propositions <a href="#prop:observable" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:observable">48</a> and <a href="#prop:holomorphy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:holomorphy">50</a>, and Lemma <a href="#lemma:boundary-uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:boundary-uniqueness">51</a>). This common boundary problem identifies every subsequential crossing limit. A deterministic comparison at fixed buffers in a Schoenflies chart then transfers this crossing law to the exact closed-cell event in arbitrary Jordan domains.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### A quenched consequence for rectangles

<a id="stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles"></a>
<div id="cor:quenched-rectangles" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Quenched Cardy limit for fixed rectangles). *Let $D$ be a fixed bounded nondegenerate open rectangle and let $a,b,c,d$ be its corners in counterclockwise order, with $ab$ and $cd$ the chosen opposite sides. Write $x_D$ for its conformal parameter above and set $$Q_\varepsilon(\eta)=\mathbb P^{\eta}\bigl(\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d)\bigr),
 \qquad \eta=X_\varepsilon.$$ If $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}$ and $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}$ denote probability and expectation for the Poisson geometry at mesh $\varepsilon$, then $$\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}\bigl[|Q_\varepsilon-F(x_D)|^2\bigr]=0.$$ In particular, for every $t>0$, $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}(|Q_\varepsilon-F(x_D)|>t)\to0$. These limits use the geometry law at each mesh, without a coupling across meshes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-quenched-rectangles</summary>

*Proof.* Almost surely only finitely many Voronoi cells meet $\overline D$, and each cell clipped to $\overline D$ is closed and convex. Every connected component of a finite union of such clipped cells is path connected, by concatenating segments through a chain of intersecting cells. Thus, almost surely in the geometry and for every coloring, our connected-set crossing event agrees with the continuous closed-cell crossing event in (Vanneuville 2025, Definition 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Vanneuville2025](#ref-Vanneuville2025)].

After an orientation-preserving isometry, write $\overline D=[-w,w]\times[-h,h]$, with the chosen sides vertical. Scaling by $\varepsilon^{-1}$ sends the full-plane intensity-$\varepsilon^{-2}$ Poisson process to unit intensity and this rectangle to $[-\lambda R,\lambda R]\times[-R,R]$, where $\lambda=w/h$ and $R=h/\varepsilon$. The Voronoi cells and their conditional fair coloring commute with this similarity. Therefore (Vanneuville 2025, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Vanneuville2025](#ref-Vanneuville2025)] gives an absolute $\alpha>0$ and a constant $C_\lambda<\infty$ such that, for $0<\varepsilon\le h$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}(Q_\varepsilon)
 \le C_\lambda(\varepsilon/h)^\alpha.$$ Let $m_\varepsilon=\mathbb P(\mathcal C_\varepsilon(D;a,b,c,d))$. Conditioning on the geometry gives $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}Q_\varepsilon=m_\varepsilon$, while Theorem <a href="#thm:cardy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cardy">1</a> gives $m_\varepsilon\to F(x_D)$. Hence $$\mathbb E_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}|Q_\varepsilon-F(x_D)|^2
 =\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mathrm{geom},\varepsilon}(Q_\varepsilon)+|m_\varepsilon-F(x_D)|^2
 \le C_\lambda(\varepsilon/h)^\alpha+|m_\varepsilon-F(x_D)|^2
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Markov’s inequality gives the assertion in probability. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Geometry, bulk patches and notation

Write $\omega=\exp(2\pi\mathrm i/3)$. Unless specified otherwise, sites, edges and triangles belong to the primal Delaunay triangulation. Either color can be the path color; we describe it as black when making a construction. Distances, diameters and balls are Euclidean, and complex vectors use the positive orientation of the plane.

We recall the geometric facts behind this convention. Almost surely, the Poisson process is locally finite and has neither collinear triples nor cocircular quadruples, by diffuseness and a countable exhaustion. For a finite set in general position, empty-circumdisk triangles form the triangulation of its convex hull: lift the sites to the paraboloid and project the lower faces. Their circumcenters are Voronoi vertices, and shared cell sides correspond to primal edges. The local determination estimate in Lemma <a href="#lemma:mesh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:mesh">12</a> makes these finite constructions stabilize on every compact set along an exhaustion. It also bounds the cells of sites in any fixed compact set. Thus the full-plane cells form a locally finite polygonal tiling with trivalent meeting points. Sites of touching cells are primal neighbors, including when the cells meet at a vertex. The primal graph can equivalently be drawn through the tiling by joining sites to interior points of shared cell sides. This drawing has the same planar orders as the straight Delaunay drawing.

<a id="stmt-def-bulk-setting"></a>
<div id="def:bulk-setting" class="definition">

**Definition 3** (Bulk setting). A bulk setting is either the full-plane sample or a finite simple triangulation of the sphere carrying independent fair site colors conditional on its graph and geometry. In the latter case an oriented open planar patch is coupled to a full-plane sample so that, on every fixed compact subset of the patch, the graphs and their colors agree with probability tending to one as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The graph and chart agreement event is independent of color sampling conditional on the geometry. Spatial weights are used only on triangles in the agreement patch. Compactly supported tests may be set to zero on the exceptional geometry event.

</div>

The finite completions will be useful when the observable is formed near the boundary. No geometric control of their triangles outside the patch is required for the local field identities.

We use the Jordan–Schoenflies theorem in its prescribed-boundary form (Moise 1977, Theorem 10.4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Moise1977](#ref-Moise1977)], its extension to a simple planar arc (Antoine 1921, Première Partie, Chapitre I, Section 17) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Antoine1921](#ref-Antoine1921)], and Carathéodory’s boundary extension theorem. We also use the following form of the Radó convergence theorem (Radó 1923, sec. 1, pp. 182–183) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Rado1923](#ref-Rado1923)]. If Jordan boundaries have parametrizations converging uniformly and in order to a Jordan boundary, their Riemann maps, normalized at a common interior point and with positive derivative, converge uniformly in the closed-disk parametrization to the limiting chart. When a prescribed ordered cut is to be realized by an ambient change, we give the extension argument explicitly in Lemma <a href="#lemma:exact-fit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:exact-fit">39</a>. Paths, band sides and gates are always considered together with their planar embeddings.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Reading the proof and the order of limits

Section <a href="#sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fields">2</a> establishes the exact fields and the stationary closed-sum estimate. Section <a href="#sec:probability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:probability">3</a> states the percolation inputs and derives the local arm and mesh estimates. Sections <a href="#sec:plugs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:plugs">4</a> and <a href="#sec:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine">5</a> construct the state transport and its affine decomposition. Sections <a href="#sec:pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pins">6</a> and <a href="#sec:actual-arcs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:actual-arcs">7</a> place plugs on actual rims and prove the bulk transport theorem. Section <a href="#sec:nondegeneracy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nondegeneracy">8</a> excludes its degenerate scalar. Section <a href="#sec:observable" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:observable">9</a> constructs the holomorphic limit and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:cardy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cardy">1</a>.

Throughout the transport construction, the microscopic limit $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ is taken first, at a fixed comparison scale $h$ and for finitely many fixed patterns. Pattern-specific positive conditioning probabilities may be small. Bounds asserted to be uniform of order $h$ use only a fixed finite family of short normalized templates. Spatial refinement depth is also fixed during the microscopic limit and is subsequently increased before $h$ decreases. The final Jordan-domain buffers are fixed until the microscopic crossing limit has been taken. Each argument below specifies which of these limits is in use.

<a id="sec:fields"></a>
### Circuit components and orthogonal fields

The finite identity in this section is independent of the geometry of a triangulation. Geometry enters only when we assign displacement vectors to its sides. A component beside a dual circuit, rather than the whole side of that circuit, is the coordinate of the identity. We first obtain the separate second-moment identities for the two displacement fields and then use stationarity to control their complete macroscopic rim sums.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### An exact identity on a triangulated sphere

Fix a finite simple triangulation $T$ of the oriented sphere, and give its vertices independent fair colors. Draw its trivalent dual $\Gamma$ through the interiors of the triangles. A dual edge is *occupied* when the endpoints of the primal edge it crosses have different colors. At a dual vertex there are either zero or two occupied edges. The occupied subgraph is therefore a union of vertex-disjoint simple circuits.

In fact its law is uniform on the even subgraphs of $\Gamma$. To see this, prescribe a set of primal edges across which the spin changes. Spins can be recovered, starting from one vertex, precisely when the number of prescribed changes around every primal cycle is even. The face boundaries generate the cycle space of a spherical triangulation, so it is enough to check the triangular faces. These are exactly the even-degree conditions in the dual. Every allowable prescription has the two spin assignments obtained from one another by interchanging the colors.

For each occupied circuit $L$, take the connected components $C$ of the topological graph $\Gamma\setminus L$: all points of $L$, including its vertices, are removed. In particular, an open edge whose two endpoints have been removed can be a component by itself. Figure <a href="#fig:component-labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:component-labels">1</a> illustrates the deletion convention in the tetrahedral dual. Put $\lambda=(L,C)$ and, for a point $m$ in the interior of a dual edge, define $$H_\lambda(m)=\mathbf 1_{\{m\in C\}}.$$ Vectors indexed by these pairs, with the index set taken separately in each configuration, have the scalar product $$\langle F,G\rangle
   =\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}\sum_\lambda F_\lambda\overline{G_\lambda}.$$ Here and throughout the finite discussion, $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{sp}}$ averages colors with the triangulation fixed. Write $H(m)=(H_\lambda(m))_\lambda$ and $K(m,n)=\langle H(m),H(n)\rangle$.

<figure id="fig:component-labels">

<figcaption>Components in the tetrahedral dual $Γ = K_{4}$. The heavy red circuit $L$ and its hollow vertices are shown for reference but are deleted. The remaining blue graph has one component in the left panel and two in the right panel; its endpoints at deleted vertices are excluded. Components are determined by connectivity in $Γ \ L$.</figcaption>
</figure>
